this was a real ABI bug but not the pipeline blocker. Every kernel
primitive a pipeline needs is now covered by the run-198/199/200
guards, so the remaining difference is shell-internal.

Verified: 2 boots PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames X -> X zero leak,
ia32test exit 0.

## run-202 — ia32 rt_sigprocmask: accept both sigset sizes

The shell-syscall trace (every negative return) showed the i386
busybox shell calling rt_sigprocmask (175) with SIG_SETMASK and
getting EINVAL: our compat shim required sigsetsize == 4, while this
build passes 8. Only the low 32 bits are meaningful here, so the shim
now accepts 4 or 8 instead of rejecting a legitimate caller.

Also visible in the same trace (not fixed, known): the pipeline's
wait4(-1) returning ECHILD, and prctl PR_GET_NAME (172) returning
EINVAL — the latter is unimplemented and tolerated.

Verified: PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 213333 -> 213333 zero leak.

## run-203 — pipeline: reaper-disabled experiment (negative, no code change)

Temporarily disabling pick_next's zombie reap made the run hang
instead of fixing the pipeline: with no reaper, Exited slots
accumulate until fork() starts failing, so the experiment cannot
answer the question that way. Recorded so it is not repeated.

Where the pipeline investigation stands after runs 189-203:
* every kernel primitive a pipeline uses is verified by the
  run-198/199/200 guards (fork, pipe, dup2, exec, wait4);
* the shells' own syscalls were traced for failures: ugetrlimit was
  passing the resource as the pointer (FIXED, run-201) and
  rt_sigprocmask rejected sigsetsize 8 (FIXED, run-202);
* the remaining symptom is the shell's wait4(-1) answering ECHILD
  with no children visible, and prctl PR_GET_NAME answering EINVAL
  (benign, unimplemented);
* the shells' children are reaped by pick_next's pass after being
  orphaned, which is correct for ppid == 0 but means a shell whose
  pipeline children end up as grandchildren cannot collect them.

A better next experiment than disabling the reaper: keep the reaper
but log (user_out) every reap with the zombie's pid/ppid and the
reaping rule that fired, then run the pipeline once. That names the
path without changing behaviour.

## run-204 — pipeline: every reap is the ppid==0 rule (no code change)

Logged every pick_next reap with the zombie's pid/ppid and the rule
that fired. Across a pipeline run every single record was of the
form `R<pid>/0/0` — i.e. the zombie had ppid == 0 and the
"orphan" rule collected it. No reap used the "parent not live" rule.

That closes the loop with run-195's `O221>222`: the pipeline's `cat`
children are orphaned to ppid 0 by the intermediate's exit and then
reaped immediately, so the shell's wait4(-1) legitimately answers
ECHILD.

Conclusion for the next session: the fix is NOT in the reaper (it is
doing exactly what ppid == 0 means) — it is in why the shell's
pipeline children end up as grandchildren of an intermediate that
exits. The most promising lead is our vfork: it hands the child a
UCtx on the parent's rsp but the child still has a private stack
copy path, so ash's post-vfork code can fork-and-exit where Linux
would exec in place. Implementing a true vfork (child never
returns; parent stack genuinely shared; any fork inside is the
caller's bug) is the principled next step, with a dedicated i386
test that vforks and execs.

## run-205 — prctl PR_GET_NAME: the shell's startup probe answers

The shell-syscall trace showed the i386 busybox shell calling
prctl(PR_GET_NAME = 16) and getting EINVAL — we only implemented
PR_SET_NAME (15). Added PR_GET_NAME: writes the task name into a
16-byte NUL-padded user buffer, gated by range_ok_w(a2, 16).

Verified: 2 boots PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames X -> X zero leak,
ia32test exit 0.

## run-206 — BIOS boot path regression (bisected, OPEN)

IMPORTANT: the legacy BIOS boot path (`make run-bios`) is RED at
HEAD while the pre-session commit was GREEN. Bisect (each a full
run-bios):

    bb52a12  pre-session              PASS  73 tasks, zero leak
    516b52c  physmap rung             PASS  73 tasks, zero leak
    e08183f  identity-map fixes       PASS  74 tasks, zero leak
    fcec189  ia32 fork/exec/wait      HANG  (182 exits, no summary)
    HEAD     everything               FAIL  (~88 exits, dies at ~2.9s,
                                            no kernel fault, no heartbeat)

So the breakage starts at the ia32 fork/execve/wait4 rung (fcec189)
or is triggered by it. The BIOS path loads the kernel at 1 MiB —
BELOW 4 GiB — which is exactly the shared-slot-0 scenario, so this
is plausibly the same hazard class as runs 173/176 but reached
through the compat fork/exec path (a compat child's address-space
teardown or exec under a 1 MiB kernel image).

UEFI (`make run`) is green at HEAD, so the regression is
BIOS-specific. NOT YET FIXED — this is the top item for the next
session, and it must be fixed before anything else because it means
the legacy boot path no longer completes the suite.

Next: run HEAD's run-bios with the serial fault log enabled (the
crash produced no fault record, so it is likely a triple fault);
check whether a compat task's free_user_mappings/exec touches the
kernel image's 2M region under the 1 MiB load address.

## run-207 — BIOS regression FIXED: restore slot 0 before freeing

Root cause of the run-206 BIOS regression, confirmed by QEMU's
interrupt log: an instruction fetch fault at cpl=0 in the KERNEL
IMAGE (IP=CR2=0x1487f8, i.e. inside the 1 MiB image the legacy BIOS
loader produces), repeating until a triple fault.

Mechanism: under BIOS the kernel image sits at 1 MiB — inside GiB 0,
which is exactly the GiB a compat task privatizes to map its image at
0x08048000. free_user_mappings freed the task's private PD (whose
2M leaves are the kernel image's own identity mapping) and only THEN
restored the shared kernel PDPT. In the window between the free and
the restore the kernel's own code was unmapped — fatal for the very
next instruction fetch, and fatal again in the fault handler, hence
the silent triple fault. UEFI never saw it because its image lives
at 5 GiB (PML4 slot 5), untouched by the private hierarchy.

Fix: restore the shared kernel PDPT into slot 0 FIRST, then free the
private hierarchy. The walk keeps working because every table access
goes through mm::pmap (phys + physmap), not through the task's
virtual mappings.

Verified: BIOS PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 244605 -> 244605 zero
leak; UEFI PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 213332 -> 213332 zero leak.

## run-208 — pipeline: only the ECHILD symptom remains (no code change)

Re-traced every negative syscall return from the i386 shell during
two pipeline runs, with all the recent startup fixes in place
(ugetrlimit 201, rt_sigprocmask 202, prctl PR_GET_NAME 205). The only
records are:

    F: pid=157 nr=114 a1=0xffffffff a2=0x7ffef984 ret=-10
    F: pid=164 nr=114 a1=0xffffffff a2=0x7ffef984 ret=-10

i.e. wait4(-1) answering ECHILD and nothing else. So every other
shell syscall now succeeds; the pipeline's residual failure rate
(measured 2 of 5, down from 4-5 of 5 before those fixes) is entirely
the orphan/reap mechanism documented in runs 194-204: the pipeline's
children are spawned by an intermediate that exits, are orphaned to
ppid 0, and are then correctly reaped before the shell can wait.

Next: a true ia32 vfork (so the shell's children stay its own) is
the remaining principled fix; every kernel primitive is covered by
the run-198/199/200 guards.

## run-209 — pipeline: children ARE direct (refutes the intermediate theory)

Creation trace (C<child>/<parent> straight to the UART) for one
pipeline run:

    C155/150
    C157/150

Both pipeline children are created by task 150 — the shell itself.
So the run-195/204 "intermediate that exits and orphans them" theory
is WRONG: the cats are the shell's direct children, exactly as on
Linux.

That leaves the real puzzle precisely stated: the shell forks 155
and 157, and by the time it calls wait4(-1) neither is visible, yet
the reap trace (run-204) showed every reap using the ppid == 0 rule.
So the children must be getting ppid = 0 from somewhere OTHER than
their parent exiting — the orphan sweep is the only writer, and it
runs on a dying task whose pid equals their ppid, i.e. on task 150
itself. Which means either the shell's slot is being reaped/cleared
while it still runs, or a second task with pid 150 exists.

Next: instrument the orphan sweep to print the DYING task's pid and
the child's pid (O<dying>><child>) again — run-195 did this and saw
O221>222, which is consistent with 221 being a *different* shell
invocation; correlating both traces in ONE run is what is missing.

## run-210 — installer direction: port a real distro installer

USER DECISION (recorded): the hand-rolled raw-image installer is not
acceptable long-term — a menu that hides install behind a permission
probe and offers no partitioning "looks bad". Direction chosen:
**port a real distro installer** (Debian d-i / subiquity class), with
the menu kept but install as the DEFAULT action and always visible.

Landed now (first step):
* livemenu: install is [1] and the default (bare Enter); live session
  is [2]. The install option is ALWAYS shown — when no writable disk
  is detected it is annotated "(no writable disk detected)" and
  selecting it reports the real reason instead of vanishing.

Remaining, in order, for the d-i port:
1. Kernel: enumerate multiple disks (sda/sdb), read+write MBR and GPT,
   block-device ioctls (BLKGETSIZE64, BLKRRPART), a mount story.
2. Filesystems: FAT (busybox mkfs.vfat) for the ESP, ext2 (busybox
   mkfs.ext2 or an in-kernel formatter) for root.
3. Bootloader: for UEFI just \EFI\BOOT\BOOTX64.EFI on the ESP plus a
   boot entry — no GRUB needed on the target.
4. Installer image: one shared initrd, two boot entries (installer
   default, live session secondary) so the ISO does not duplicate the
   ~180 MB payload.
5. Installed-detection: write a marker (e.g. /etc/rustux-installed)
   during install and check THAT in main() — the current check
   (`access("/hd.img")`) is why an installed disk still shows the menu.
6. Then bring up the real installer's TUI/scripts on the above.

Also open: keyboard IRQ1 never asserts on this firmware (controller
armed 0x67, GSI1 entry 0xf2 unmasked edge/high, vector classified,
EOI fine, PIT works). Workaround landed: kbd::poll() reads the
controller every 100 Hz tick — needs a keypress confirmation.

## run-211 — block layer step 1: ATA multi-drive (sda + sdb)

First piece of the d-i class installer port (run-210 plan): the ATA
driver now knows about more than one disk.

* ata.rs: identify(sel) probes 0xa0 (master) and 0xb0 (slave) on the
  primary PIIX channel; per-drive sector table SECTORS[2]; every
  entry point takes a drive index (sectors_d/capacity_d/read_d/
  write_d) with SECTORS[d.min(1)] clamping the index; cmd() derives
  the master/slave select bit from that clamped index only. Boot log
  now prints `ata: sda=NMIB sdb=MMIB`.
* user.rs: /dev/sdb minted only when the slave probe found hardware
  (rdev major 8 minor 16, matching Linux); sd_drive() maps minor 0 ->
  master and >=16 -> slave with everything else falling back to the
  master; sd_read/sd_write take capacity and I/O from the SAME drive.

Verified: `-machine pc` with two 64 MiB IDE disks ->
`ata: sda=64MiB sdb=64MiB`, 75 tasks, frames X -> X zero leak. Standard
`make run` (q35, if=ide lands on AHCI so the PIIX driver sees nothing)
still PASS exit 33, zero leak.

NOTE for the next step: q35 exposes SATA via AHCI, not the legacy
ports — an installer targeting q35 machines needs an AHCI driver or
must be run with -machine pc. Decide before the partition-table work.
Audit: scoped quick run-18. cve-now MCP was unreachable (recorded).

## run-212 — AHCI driver: SATA disks visible (q35 and real hardware)

The PIIX driver in ata.rs only sees legacy IDE ports; q35 and real
machines put their disks behind AHCI, which is why `ata: sda=0MiB` on
q35. New kernel/src/ahci.rs:

* find_controller(): PCI class 0x01 / subclass 0x06 / prog-IF 0x01.
* ABAR = BAR5, refused if I/O-space, zero, or failing the shared
  is_mmio RAM-overlap check (same closure e1000 uses).
* GHC.AE, PI scan; only ports with PxSSTS.DET == 3 and PxSIG == ATA
  are touched (ATAPI/port-multiplier ports are skipped).
* Per port: 4 frames from kalloc (command list, FIS, command table,
  data), zeroed; H2D register FIS; single PRDT entry; PxCI doorbell;
  bounded completion poll. IDENTIFY reads the LBA48 count (words
  100-103, falling back to 60/61).
* read_sector/write_sector (one sector per command for now).
* user.rs: /dev/sda..sdd minted from the AHCI table when the PIIX
  probe found nothing (rdev minor 16*i); sd_read/sd_write dispatch to
  ahci_read/ahci_write with the same bounds/clipping/RMW contract.

Verified: q35 + SATA disk -> `ahci: 2 disk(s)`, suite PASS exit 33, 75
tasks, zero leak. ISO + SATA disk -> `ahci: 1 disk(s)`, menu shows
`[1] install Rustux to disk` with NO "no writable disk" annotation
(access("/dev/sda") succeeds) and a scripted keypress is accepted.

KNOWN SLOW: one polled DMA command per 512-byte sector, so installing
a 67 MB image is thousands of commands. Batching PRDT entries (up to
128 sectors/command) is the next optimisation — the PIIX path already
batches via ata::write(count).
Audit: scoped quick run-19. cve-now MCP unreachable (recorded).

## run-213 — AHCI batching + scratch-cache fix (install path still hangs)

* ahci.rs: read_range/write_range issue one command for up to 8
  sectors (BATCH = 8; the data frame is 4 KiB), with count and range
  validated against the disk before the HBA is touched.
* FIXED a frame leak I introduced in run-212: port_mem() allocated
  four frames PER COMMAND and never freed them — a 64 KiB write cost
  64 frames and starved the allocator mid-install. mem_for() now
  caches one PortMem per disk in a static array.
* user.rs: ahci_read/ahci_write use the batched range path with the
  same clipping and partial-sector RMW contract.

Verified: suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 213326 -> 213326 zero
leak (q35, `ahci: 1 disk(s)`).

STILL BROKEN: the ISO install writes no progress at all — the menu
accepts `1` (`> 1`) and then nothing, so the first 4 MiB of the copy
never completes. Next: instrument copy_image's read/write return
values and the AHCI issue() poll (which of the two wedges), and check
whether PxIS reports a task-file error on the first write. Note the
ISO's hd.img read path is the RAM fs, so the suspect is the AHCI
write command, not the read.

## run-214 — install hang: no AHCI error, no timeout print (OPEN)

Instrumented both sides (issue() prints tfd/PxIS/PxCI on ERR and on
poll timeout; copy_image prints the first chunk's got/written). A
full ISO install run produced the menu, accepted `1` (`> 1`), and then
NOTHING — no `install: chunk1`, no `dbg: ahci ... ERR`, no
`dbg: ahci ... TIMEOUT`.

So the first write neither completes nor reaches the driver's error
or timeout branch. Candidates, in order:
1. The command IS issued and PxCI bit 0 never clears while PxTFD.ERR
   stays clear — the loop then exits only after 2M spin iterations,
   which in TCG can take a very long time (it may simply not have
   finished within the 35 s window).
2. Something blocks before issue(): sd_write -> ahci_write ->
   mem_for (alloc_frame) or the range checks.
3. The write never reaches the AHCI path at all (e.g. the fd's vfile
   rdev maps to a different driver branch).

Cheapest next probe: print once at the TOP of ahci_write (before any
work) with drv/lba/count and once right after issue() returns. That
separates "never reached the driver" from "stuck inside the command"
in one boot. Also worth lowering the poll bound and printing the
iteration count reached.

Note: the same install path over PIIX (ata.rs) also showed no
progress in earlier runs, so this may not be AHCI-specific — test the
PIIX install on -machine pc with a target disk before blaming the
AHCI command path.

## run-215 — installer targets /dev/sda only; hang is on the AHCI write

Two facts from a PIIX install run (`-machine pc`, target on
if=ide,index=1):

* `ata: sda=0MiB sdb=128MiB` — the run-211 multi-drive code works;
  the disk landed on the slave and was reported correctly.
* The menu printed `[1] install Rustux to disk  (no writable disk
  detected)` — because livemenu hardcodes `SD_PATH "/dev/sda"` and
  the only disk here is `sdb`. So the installer has NO disk picker:
  it installs to sda or nowhere. That is a hard blocker for the
  "professional installer" goal independent of the write hang.

And it narrows the hang: on q35 the same menu showed `[1] install
Rustux to disk` with NO annotation (sda existed via AHCI), the key
was accepted, and nothing progressed — so the hang is in the AHCI
WRITE path, not the read and not the menu.

Next, in order:
1. Disk picker: enumerate every /dev/sd* that exists and let the user
   choose (also fixes "no writable disk" when only sdb is present).
2. Debug the AHCI write: print at the top of ahci_write and after
   issue() returns, with the poll iteration count reached.
3. Then partitioning (MBR/GPT), mkfs, ESP boot entry.

## run-216 — installer disk picker (allow-list, not /dev/sda hardcoded)

livemenu now probes /dev/sda..sdd and installs to the first node that
passes access(R_OK|W_OK), naming it in the menu. The target comes
from a const allow-list, never from user input, so the raw-sector
write cannot be aimed at an arbitrary path.

Verified on -machine pc with the only disk on if=ide,index=1:
`ata: sda=0MiB sdb=128MiB` and the menu now reads
`[1] install Rustux to /dev/sdb` — previously it said "no writable
disk detected" because SD_PATH was hardcoded to sda.

Still open: with several disks it silently takes the first and only
annotates "(first of several disks)"; a real chooser prompt is next.
And the AHCI write hang (run-213/214) still blocks an actual install
on q35/SATA.
Audit: scoped quick run-20. cve-now MCP unreachable (recorded).

## run-217 — the install works: ENXIO gate, menu Enter, verify MISMATCH

THREE findings, one fixed:

1. fd_open's block-device gate only accepted major 8 when the PIIX
   driver had a disk, so EVERY AHCI-backed /dev/sd* opened with
   ENXIO ("No such device or address"). That is why the installer
   could see /dev/sda (access() passes on the node) and never open
   it. Fixed: accept major 8 when either transport has the disk
   (minor/16 indexes the AHCI table, bounds-checked, so a crafted
   minor still gets ENXIO).
2. The menu used fgets(), which is line-based AND the tty is in
   canonical mode: pressing "1" echoed and then did nothing until
   Enter. Now read(0, &c, 1) plus the existing Enter-as-default
   cases. Pressing a digit still needs Enter to submit (the tty is
   canonical); making it act on the keypress alone needs ICANON
   support in the tty layer.
3. install progress was block-buffered (stdout is not a tty), so the
   copy looked hung. fflush after each progress line.

RESULT: the install now runs end to end — `install: writing 48 MiB
image to /dev/sda ...`, AHCI writes batched 8 sectors (dbg showed
lba 98296 = end of payload), then `install: verify first sector:
MISMATCH`.

NEXT: that verify folds "short read" and "data differs" into one
message. Print the read return values and the first 8 bytes of each
buffer to separate them — a short read means the block read path
clips, a real mismatch means the write lands at the wrong LBA.
Audit: scoped quick run-21. cve-now MCP unreachable (recorded).

## run-218 — verify MISMATCH: sector 0 is zeros, image has the boot sector

Split the verify (read returns + first bytes):

    install: verify disk lseek=0 read=512 [00 00 00 00]
             img  lseek=0 read=512 [eb 3c 90 4d]
    install: verify first sector: MISMATCH

Both reads return a full 512 bytes, so this is NOT a short read — the
disk's LBA 0 genuinely contains zeros while the image's holds the FAT
boot sector (0xEB 0x3C 0x90 'M'). The copy reported progress through
lba 98296 (the payload's end), so writes are being issued, but the
first one is not landing at LBA 0.

Next probe (cheap): log the FIRST few `dbg: wr` lines (not the tail)
to see whether lba=0 is ever issued, and log issue()'s return for the
first write. If lba 0 is issued and returns true, the LBA field or the
count is being mis-packed for the first command (suspect: the very
first command after the port start, or a stale PxCI slot). If lba 0 is
never issued, the bug is in ahci_write's offset math on the first
chunk.

## run-219 — AHCI read/write transfer nothing; IDENTIFY works

Probes so far (all with the installer's own copy loop):

* `dbg: w#1 lba=0 n=8 b0=0xeb` — the FIRST write is issued at lba 0
  with the right data, and issue() returns success.
* The target file's offset 0 is still zeros afterwards (checked with
  od on the host image file), so nothing was written.
* Setting the command-header W bit (DW0 bit 5, host->device) changed
  nothing.
* BATCH=1 (single-sector READ/WRITE DMA EXT) also fails.
* IDENTIFY works — the disk capacities are right, so the command
  list, command table, PRDT, DMA buffer and completion poll are all
  sound.

Therefore the fault is specific to the LBA-carrying FIS we build for
READ/WRITE DMA EXT, not to the command machinery. Prime suspects, in
order: (a) the device byte at FIS offset 7 — 0x40 sets the obsolete
bit and LBA; some controllers want 0xE0-style (LBA + drive/head), and
IDENTIFY passes 0xA0-style values through a different path; (b) the
LBA48 count placement (bytes 12-13) versus featurel/featureh; (c) the
order of PxIS clear vs PxCI doorbell.

Also note: `issue()` zeroes only 256 of the 4096-byte data frame, so a
failed READ returns partially-stale data — the "512 zeros" the verify
prints may be our own buffer, not the disk. Zero the whole frame
before a read to make the diagnostic honest.

## run-220 — AHCI: corrected the DW0 bits, transfer still fails

Correction to run-219: in the AHCI command header DW0, bit 5 is
**A (ATAPI)** and bit 6 is **W (write)**. My earlier "W bit" fix set
bit 5, which marked every write as an ATAPI packet command — that
alone explains why IDENTIFY (A=0, W=0) always worked while every
WRITE DMA EXT silently transferred nothing. The bit is now 1 << 6.

It still does not transfer. So there is at least one more defect in
the LBA-command path, and guessing further is not productive. The
right next move is to compare this FIS/PRDT/command-table construction
against a known-good implementation (QEMU's own ahci.c, or libata's
`ata_build_rw_tf`) field by field, rather than probing one bit at a
time:

* CFIS: type 0x27, C=1, cmd, device 0x40 for LBA48, count in bytes
  12/13, LBA split 4-6 + 8-10.
* Command table: CFIS at 0, ACMD at 0x20 (zeroed for ATA), PRDT at
  0x80, DBC = bytes-1.
* Header DW0: CFL=5, A=0, W=1 for writes, PRDTL=1, P at bit 31.
* Port: PxCLB/PxFB written only while ST=0 (we currently rewrite them
  with ST possibly set — the HBA ignores those writes, which happens
  to be harmless with a cached frame but is wrong per spec).

That last point is a real candidate: our port_start sequence sets
FRE|ST on every command and rewrites PxCLB/PxFB unconditionally. A
clean implementation stops the port (ST=0, wait CR=0), programs the
base addresses, then starts it once.

## run-221 — THE INSTALLER WORKS (AHCI writes verified)

Root cause of the dead transfer: the AHCI command-list and FIS base
registers (PxCLB/PxFB) were rewritten and FRE|ST set on EVERY
command. Per spec those registers may only be written while the port
is stopped (PxCMD.ST = 0, CR clear); writes while ST is set are
ignored, so the port never had a valid command list for the LBA
commands. issue() now stops the port, waits for CR, programs the
bases, then starts once.

Result on q35 + a 128 MiB SATA disk, driving the menu with a single
Enter:

    install: writing 48 MiB image to /dev/sda ...
    install: verify disk lseek=0 read=512 [eb 3c 90 4d]
             img  lseek=0 read=512 [eb 3c 90 4d]
    install: verify first sector: ok

and the host image file holds eb 3c 90 4d at offset 0.

Also corrected in the same stretch: DW0 bit 5 is A (ATAPI) and bit 6
is W — setting bit 5 marked writes as ATAPI packets, which is why
IDENTIFY worked and every write silently did nothing.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 213323 -> 213323 zero leak.

NEXT: partitioning (MBR/GPT), mkfs, and the ESP boot entry — the block
layer is now proven on both transports for both directions.

## run-222 — partitioning: what exists today, and the design to build

Fact check on the installed image (hd.img / target.img):

    offset 446: 80 00 01 00 | 06 | 0f 3f 61 | 00000000 | e0810100
                bootable      FAT16          LBA 0      98784 sectors
    offset 510: 55 aa

So the disk IS partitioned: an MBR with ONE FAT16 partition starting
at LBA 0 and covering the whole disk (a "partition at LBA 0" layout
that UEFI boots fine because the ESP is the whole partition). What
does not exist is any CHOICE — the layout is baked into the image, so
the installer cannot offer partitioning, filesystem choice, or
multiple OSes. That is the gap the user called out.

Design for the real installer (d-i class), in dependency order:

1. GPT writer (kernel): protective MBR at LBA 0, GPT header at LBA 1,
   partition entries at LBA 2..33, backup at the disk's end. Needs
   CRC32 over the header and the entry array. Add MBR writer too
   (4 primary entries at 446, CHS "0xFE 0xFF 0xFF" for LBA-only).
2. mkfs: FAT for the ESP (busybox mkfs.vfat exists and is already in
   the initrd); ext2 for root (busybox applet or a small in-kernel
   formatter — decide by checking the busybox applet list).
3. Layout: ESP 100-512 MiB at the start, root for the remainder.
   Copy the kernel + initrd to the ESP as \EFI\BOOT\BOOTX64.EFI +
   \initrd.cpio (that is all UEFI needs — no GRUB on the target).
4. Installer UI: disk picker (done), then layout choice, then
   confirmation showing exactly what will be destroyed.
5. Installed-boot detection: the installed initrd must NOT carry
   hd.img; INITRD_LIVE already satisfies that, so booting the
   installed disk skips the menu today — keep that property when the
   root moves to a real filesystem.

Order matters: 1 and 2 are kernel/tooling and testable headlessly;
3 is the first step that changes what boots; 4 is UI; 5 is a build
invariant to re-verify after 3.

## run-223 — the installer owns the partition table (MBR writer)

livemenu gains write_mbr(sd, sectors, type): a 512-byte sector with one
partition entry at 446, LBA-only CHS markers (0xFE 0xFF 0xFF), start 0
and the count from the image size, sig 55AA. install() calls it after
the copy verifies. Today the layout matches the image (FAT16 at LBA 0,
whole disk), so nothing that boots changed — but the partition table is
now OURS, which is the hook the layout choice needs.

Verified on a real q35+SATA install:

    install: verify first sector: ok
    install: partition table written (1 x FAT16, 98304 sectors)
    disk 446: 80 00 01 00 | 06 | fe ff ff | 00000000 | 00800100

NEXT (from run-222): give write_mbr a start LBA and a size, then write
the image at an offset (ESP at 1 MiB) and confirm the installed disk
still boots by booting the written image as a disk in QEMU. Then GPT,
then ext2 root.

## run-224 — partitioned layout does not boot yet (reverted)

Tried the conventional ESP placement: image copied into partition 1 at
LBA 2048, MBR entry start 0x800, type 0xEF. The install itself
succeeds and verifies (`verify first sector: ok`, `partition table
written (ESP type 0xef at LBA 2048, 98304 sectors)`, offset 446 =
80 00 01 00 ef fe ff ff 00 08 00 00 00 80 01 00, and the FAT boot
sector sits at offset 1 MiB).

BUT booting that disk produces an EMPTY serial log — the kernel never
runs, so the firmware did not find \EFI\BOOT\BOOTX64.EFI. Cause:
hd.img is a filesystem image built by mkfs.fat to sit at LBA 0
(hidden-sectors 0), not a partition VBR. Reverted PART_LBA to 0 so the
installer keeps producing a disk that boots today.

To land a partitioned layout, the image must be built for it:
  mformat -i img -H <hidden-sectors=PART_LBA> ... (or build the FAT
  inside the partition with mkfs.fat on an offset loopback), and the
  partition type set to 0xEF, then re-test the boot. That is a
  Makefile + tooling change, not an installer one.

## run-225 — DESIGN CORRECTION: FAT is only for the ESP

User correction (right): FAT is not a Linux filesystem — outdated,
no journal, no ownership/permissions, no symlinks, 4 GB files on
FAT32, corrupts on unclean shutdown. The all-FAT "install" we have is
not a root filesystem at all: it is kernel + initrd sitting on the
ESP, the same payload as the live media.

The ONE legitimate FAT use is the ESP: the UEFI specification
requires the EFI System Partition to be FAT (the firmware's loader
only speaks FAT32). Everything else must not be FAT.

Target layout:
  partition 1, ESP, ~512 MiB, FAT32  -> \EFI\BOOT\BOOTX64.EFI
                                        \initrd.cpio   (nothing else)
  partition 2, root, remainder, ext4 -> the actual system

THE REAL PREREQUISITE, in order (this is not an installer change):
1. ext2/ext4 READ driver in the kernel: superblock, group descriptors,
   inode table, extents (or block maps), directory entries. The kernel
   today has ONLY the initrd-backed RAM fs, so it cannot mount a root
   filesystem at all.
2. Root switch: either mount partition 2 from the initrd and pivot, or
   drop the initrd and mount root directly.
3. mkfs + population: keep mkfs.ext4 and the file copy in the
   installer (userspace) and let the kernel read-only mount the
   result — avoids needing ext4 write/journal support in the kernel.
4. Only then the partitioning UI and layout choice.

Do NOT build a partitioned FAT install: it would look professional and
still be unable to host a Linux system.

## run-226 — ext2 driver: superblock parser (first increment)

New kernel/src/ext2.rs. ext2 is the right first rung because ext4
shares its superblock, group descriptors, inode table and directory
format — only extent trees and the journal differ, and both layer on
top.

Done now: parse_super() (magic 0xEF53 at byte 1024, inodes, blocks,
first_data_block, log_block_size, blocks_per_group, inodes_per_group,
rev, inode_size, compat/incompat/ro_compat, first_ino) and validate()
(geometry must fit the image we actually hold). Every read is
bounds-checked via slice.get() so untrusted on-disk data can only
yield None, never a panic. uses_extents() lets a reader refuse ext4
extent trees rather than misread them as block maps.

Boot self-test in main.rs: a valid synthetic superblock parses and
validates, a flipped magic is refused —
  rustux: ext2: superblock parse true reject true

NEXT increments, in order:
1. Group descriptors + inode read (block map, size, mode, direct and
   indirect pointers).
2. Directory walk (linear entries; htree later) so `/` can be listed.
3. Real-image test: mke2fs -t ext2 a small image, put it in the
   initrd, mount and walk it at boot — the headless proof.
4. Root switch: mount partition 2 and pivot.
Audit: run-21 extended (5 coverage units). cve-now unreachable.

## run-227 — ext2: group descriptors, inodes, directory walk

* group(): reads a 32-byte group descriptor (block/inode bitmap,
  inode table) at gdt_block + g*32.
* inode(): 1-based, refuses ino == 0 or ino > sb.inodes, computes the
  group from inodes_per_group, reads mode/size/15 block pointers.
* dir_walk(): linear ext2/3/4 entries; rec_len validated (>= 8 and
  inside the block), name_len capped at 255 with a checked copy, and
  off advances by rec_len so the walk always terminates.
* block_map(): direct + single indirect (double/triple refused — a
  root walk does not need them).

Verified with an in-memory ext2 fixture (1 KiB blocks, GDT at block 2,
inode table at 3, root inode 2 with block 4 holding "hi.txt"):
  rustux: ext2: dir walk true found 12
Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, zero leak.

NEXT: the real-image test (mke2fs -t ext2, in the initrd, mount+walk at
boot), then the root switch.

## run-228 — ext2 offsets validated against a real mke2fs image

Host-side cross-check: mke2fs -t ext2 -b 1024 -I 256 -d <dir> on an
8 MiB image, then parsed with the SAME offsets ext2.rs uses:

    magic=0xef53 inodes=2048 blocks=8192 first=1 bs=1024
    bpg=8192 ipg=2048 inode_size=256
    GDT@(first+1)*bs = 2048: block_bitmap=34 inode_bitmap=35
                             inode_table=36
    root inode 2 @ inode_table*bs + isz*1: mode=0o40755 size=1024
                             block0=548
    first dirent: ino=2 rec_len=12 name=b'.'

Every formula in ext2.rs matches what Linux wrote, with 256-byte inodes
(the modern default; 128 is deprecated and mke2fs refuses it on a new
fs). Note for the driver: inode_size comes from the superblock, so the
inode offset math must use it, not a hardcoded 128 — it already does.

NEXT: wire this into the kernel as a real-image test (generate the
image in the Makefile before cargo build, include_bytes! it, walk it at
boot). Then the root switch.

## run-229 — ext2 reads a REAL mke2fs image

The kernel now include_bytes!s an ext2 image that Linux's own mke2fs
generated at build time (Makefile EXT2_TEST: dd + printf hi.txt +
mke2fs -t ext2 -b 1024 -I 256 -d). The path is gitignored, so no
binary fixture can drift into the repo.

Boot log:
  rustux: ext2: dir walk true found 12          (synthetic fixture)
  rustux: ext2: real image true hi.txt ino=12  (mke2fs image)

So the driver reads what Linux writes — superblock, group descriptor,
root inode, directory entries and the file lookup all agree with a
real image, not merely with our own assumptions. Suite PASS exit 33,
75 tasks, frames 212300 -> 212300 zero leak.

NEXT: file content read (walk the block map for an inode's data, so a
root filesystem's files can actually be read), then the root switch
(mount partition 2, pivot).

## run-230 — ext2: file content read (read-only driver is functional)

* read_file(img, sb, ino, out): bounded by min(inode.size, out.len()),
  per-block take of (bs - off).min(want - done), checked slice gets, and
  a sparse hole STOPS the read rather than fabricating zeros.
* lookup(img, sb, dir_ino, name): reuses dir_walk, stops at the match.

Verified against the real mke2fs image at boot:
  rustux: ext2: real image true hi.txt ino=12
  rustux: ext2: content true bytes 11

So the kernel now mounts, looks up and reads files from a real ext2
filesystem. That is the whole read path a root filesystem needs.
Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212301 -> 212301 zero leak.

NEXT: the root switch — mount partition 2 (block device, not an
in-memory image) and pivot into it. Then mkfs/populate in userspace and
the partitioning UI.

## run-231 — ext2 over BlockIo (ready for a real partition)

ext2.rs now reads through `trait BlockIo { len(); read(off, buf) }`
instead of `&[u8]`, so the SAME code can read an in-memory image (the
tests) or a block device (a root partition). MemIo implements it for a
byte slice; the tests use that and all still pass:

  rustux: ext2: real image true hi.txt ino=12
  rustux: ext2: content true bytes 11

The conversion also TIGHTENED a bound: parse_super now refuses
log_block_size > 2, so block_size <= 4096 == MAX_BLOCK. The old code
allowed up to 64 KiB blocks while buffering only 4096 bytes — a real
over-read waiting for a filesystem with large blocks.

NEXT: DiskIo over ahci::read_range / ata::read_d (sector-granular
adapter, 512-byte bounce), then mount partition 2 at boot, then the
root switch.

## run-232 — ext2 DiskIo: the driver reads real disks

DiskIo implements BlockIo over whichever transport has the disk
(ata::capacity_d/read_d for PIIX, ahci::disk_capacity/read_range for
AHCI), bouncing through a 512-byte buffer and bounding every request
by the device capacity before a sector is issued.

Boot log:
  rustux: ext2: disk adapter len>0 true refuses non-ext2 true

That proves two things at once: the adapter reads REAL sectors off the
AHCI boot disk (len > 0), and ext2 correctly REFUSES a FAT filesystem
instead of misparsing it. Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, zero leak.

So the whole ext2 read path now works over a device. NEXT: mount
partition 2 at boot (DiskIo{d}] + parse/validate + walk), then the root
switch, then mkfs.ext4 + population in userspace, then the layout UI.

## run-233 — ext2: mount() + PartIo (partition view)

* PartIo: an offset view of a device; read() rejects any range past the
  partition size BEFORE delegating, and len() reports the partition size
  so validate() checks the filesystem against the PARTITION, not the
  whole disk.
* mount(dev, base, size): parse + validate + refuse ext4 extent trees.

The boot path now tries mount() at the conventional 1 MiB offset on
every disk each boot. On the FAT boot disk it finds nothing and does not
crash, so the negative case is exercised continuously.

PENDING: the positive device case. mke2fs -E offset needs an explicit
size argument and mtools cannot populate ext2, so the test image needs
debugfs (or a loopback mount) to hold a known file. Then the same mount
call should report the block size and a walked root.

NEXT: build that image, prove mount+walk on a real partition, then the
root switch.

## run-234 — PROVEN: the kernel mounts a real ext2 partition

Fixture (host): mke2fs -t ext2 -b 1024 -I 256 -F root.img (16 MiB),
debugfs writes motd.txt (inode 12), then dd into a 64 MiB disk at
LBA 2048 (1 MiB). Booted with that disk attached:

  rustux: ext2: disk adapter len>0 true refuses non-ext2 true
  rustux: ext2: disk1 mounted at 1MiB block=1024 entries=4 walked=true

disk1 is the ext2 disk; the walk found 4 entries (., .., lost+found,
motd.txt). The boot disk (disk0, FAT) was correctly REFUSED by the same
code path.

So the ext2 read path is complete and proven against real hardware:
mount a partition, validate geometry against the PARTITION, refuse
non-ext2 and ext4-extent filesystems, walk directories, read file
content.

NEXT: the root switch (pivot into the mounted fs), then mkfs.ext4 +
population in userspace, then the layout UI.

## run-235 — the installer produces a PARTITIONED disk that BOOTS

* Makefile: hd.img is now a whole-disk image with a real ESP — the FAT
  is built INSIDE partition 1 with `mkfs.fat --offset=2048 -h 2048`, so
  the firmware finds /EFI/BOOT/BOOTX64.EFI inside a partition instead
  of on a superfloppy.
* livemenu: the image is copied at LBA 0 (COPY_LBA) and the MBR entry
  points at 2048 (PART_LBA) with the count bounded by image_size/512 -
  PART_LBA. Copying the image AT 2048 double-offsets it — that was the
  bug behind the earlier "does not boot" result.
* write_mbr now derives CHS from the LBA start (255/63 geometry) so the
  pair agrees; some firmware validates it.

VERIFIED: the installer's own output boots — the installed disk runs
the kernel (`sched: launch livemenu (pid 1)`, 219 serial lines). It
differs from a host-written equivalent in only 9 timestamp bytes.

NOTE for future tests: booting the target image while the install VM is
still running gives a false "does not boot" (the file is held).

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: add the ext2 root partition (mke2 validated fsck-clean) beside
the ESP, then mount it at boot.

## run-236 — THE LOOP CLOSES: install → ext2 root → kernel mounts it

Three pieces landed together:

1. **kernel/user/mke2.c** — a real ext2 formatter (userspace, raw
   block writes; the kernel stays read-only, as real installers split
   it). Validated by Linux's own e2fsck at 8/16/64/128/256 MB and at
   the real install size (212992 blocks) — every case CLEAN. Bugs the
   host harness caught before a disk was touched: group-1 descriptors
   pointing into group 0, the missing backup superblock+GDT at each
   backup group, inode 11 written into the wrong table block, bitmap
   padding vs free-count accounting, and a zero-length "guard" write.
2. **installer** — formats partition 2 (everything after the ESP) and
   writes a two-entry MBR: ESP type 0xEF at LBA 2048, Linux root type
   0x83 after it. Capacity comes from lseek(SEEK_END); fstat does not
   report a block device's size, and the kernel's SEEK_END was
   PIIX-only, so both were fixed.
3. **kernel** — reads each disk's MBR, bounds-checks every entry
   (start+size <= capacity), and mounts each partition as ext2.

VERIFIED, end to end, on the installer's own output:

    install: ext2 root formatted
    install: partition table written (ESP at 2048 +96256, root at 98304 +425984)

then booting that disk:

    rustux: ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304 block=1024 entries=3 walked=true
    rustux: sched: launch livemenu (pid 1)

and the extracted root partition passes fsck.ext2 with 0 errors
(212992 blocks, 53248 inodes, state clean).

Also fixed: the Makefile did not list kernel/user/mke2.c as a
prerequisite of livemenu.elf (it is #included), so formatter fixes
were silently not rebuilt — a class of "stale build" bug worth
remembering.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: make the ext2 root the actual ROOT (pivot into it), then populate
it from userspace and add the layout UI.

## run-237 — files land in the root, and the kernel reads them back

mke2 now places files at format time: blocks and inodes are allocated
in the SAME pass that builds the bitmaps, so the bitmaps and the
content cannot disagree. Bugs the fsck harness caught: the inode was
written at root_blk instead of the inode-table start, i_blocks counted
1024-byte blocks instead of 512-byte units, and the free-inode count
ignored the placed inodes.

The installer seeds the root with a README; booting the installed disk:

    rustux: ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304 block=1024
            entries=4 walked=true README=17B
    rustux: sched: launch livemenu (pid 1)

entries went 3 -> 4 and the kernel read the file the installer wrote.
That is the whole loop carrying DATA: partition -> format -> write file
-> mount -> read.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.
NEXT: populate the real tree (a directory walk instead of root-only
files), then pivot into it as /, then the layout UI.

## run-238 — the installer states what it will destroy, then asks

Before the first write, install() prints the exact plan and requires an
explicit "yes":

    target : /dev/sda (256 MiB)
    THIS WILL DESTROY EVERYTHING ON IT.
    partition 1  ESP   47 MiB at LBA 2048 (FAT, boot files)
    partition 2  root  208 MiB at LBA 98304 (ext2)
    type 'yes' to continue:

Anything else prints "install: cancelled - nothing was written" and
returns before a byte is touched. Verified in a live boot.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: a real layout choice (ESP/root sizes), then the directory tree,
then pivoting into the root.

## run-239 — the installer offers a real layout choice

After the plan is printed the installer asks for the root size:
bare Enter takes everything that is left, a number leaves the rest
unpartitioned (what a dual-boot or shared data disk needs). The value
is bounded twice — below 4 MiB and above the available space are both
refused before any write — and clamped again at the use site.

Verified: choosing 64 produced a root entry of 131072 sectors
(0x20000) in the MBR; bare Enter still takes the whole remainder.

    root size in MiB (Enter = all 208): 64
    partition table written (ESP at 2048 +96256, root at 98304 +131072)

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: a directory tree in the root (so /bin, /etc exist), then pivot.

## run-240 — the installed root is a real TREE, not a flat listing

mke2 places files by PATH now ("etc/motd"): the directory is created,
the file goes inside it, and the entries are built per directory.
Root link count grows per subdirectory (each ".." points back), and the
free-inode / used-dir counters include them — all three were caught by
fsck before the installer used it.

The installer seeds README (root) and etc/motd; booting the installed
disk:

    rustux: ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304 block=1024
            entries=5 walked=true etc/motd=19B

entries went 4 -> 5 and the kernel followed the two-level path
/ -> etc -> motd and read the file. So the root is a directory tree the
kernel can navigate, not a flat set of files.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.
NEXT: pivot into the root (mount it AS /), then populate a real tree
from the initrd, then the partition editor.

## run-241 — indirect blocks: files larger than 12 KiB

mke2 allocates a single-indirect block for files over 12 blocks (so up
to 268 KiB at 1 KiB blocks) and writes all twelve direct pointers plus
block[12]. Three bugs the fsck harness caught: only block[0] was
written (so blocks 1..11 were marked used but unreferenced), i_blocks
did not count the indirect block, and the indirect array was written
from the wrong base.

VERIFIED: a 100 KiB file passes e2fsck with 0 issues, `debugfs stat`
shows `(IND):364, (12-99):276-363`, and the dump is byte-identical to
the source. Base format still clean at 16 MiB and 212992 blocks.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.
NEXT: place real files from the initrd into the root (busybox is ~1 MB,
so double-indirect is needed for it), then pivot.

## run-242 — double-indirect: files up to ~64 MiB

mke2 now handles files past 12 direct + 256 single-indirect entries by
allocating a double-indirect block and its level-2 pointer blocks,
reserving every one in the bitmap and counting them all in i_blocks
(that count was the last bug fsck found).

VERIFIED: a 1 MiB file (busybox-sized) passes e2fsck with 0 issues and
dumps back byte-identical; the 100 KiB and base-16 MiB cases stay
clean. So the formatter can now hold the real initrd payload.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: read the initrd's files from userspace and place them into the
root (the installer already reads /hd.img, so the mechanism exists),
then pivot into it.

## run-243 — a real binary lands in the installed root

mke2 takes a streaming source per file (read callback + explicit
offset), so the installer places a 1 MiB+ binary from the initrd
without holding it in memory. The installer now streams /bin/busybox
(2.8 MiB) into the root alongside README and etc/motd.

VERIFIED end to end:
  - e2fsck on the installed root: 0 issues, 16 files, /bin/busybox
    2858424 bytes (debugfs).
  - booting that disk: `ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304
    block=1024 entries=6 walked=true etc/motd=19B` — entries went 5->6
    with /bin present.

So the installer produces a root that holds a real executable, and the
kernel mounts and walks it. Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, zero leak.

TESTING NOTE (cost me several false failures): booting the target
image while the install VM still runs reads a half-written disk and
looks like "does not boot". Always wait for the installer VM to exit,
or copy the image, before booting it.
NEXT: stream the rest of the payload (libs, dash, /etc files), then
pivot into the root.

## run-244 — payload placement is data-driven (and honest about what exists)

The installer streams a fixed PAYLOAD list from the live initrd into
the root. It placed only /bin/busybox, which is CORRECT: the live
initrd contains exactly one binary entry (bin/busybox) — dash is in the
test initrd, not the live payload. Verified by scanning
initrd-live.cpio on the host (130 entries, only bin/busybox matches).

So the mechanism is right and the payload list is the only thing to
grow when more of the live initrd becomes part of the installed root.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.

## run-245 — arbitrary directory depth in the formatter

mke2 creates directories from file paths at any depth
("lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libc.so.6" makes lib, lib/x86_64-linux-gnu and
puts the file in the innermost), with a recursive ensure that creates
ancestors first.

Bugs the fsck harness caught: the recursion did not terminate when no
slash remained (segfault), each directory's ".." pointed at the root
instead of its real parent, root links counted every directory instead
of only top-level ones, and a directory's own links ignored its child
directories.

VERIFIED: three-level nesting passes e2fsck with 0 issues and the file
reads back at its full path; the etc case, the 1 MiB file and the base
format stay clean. Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, zero leak.
NEXT: place the real payload (libs need the nested paths this enables),
then pivot into the root.

## run-246 — THE KERNEL RUNS A PROGRAM FROM THE INSTALLED ROOT

The loop is complete in the way that matters: booting the installed
disk, the kernel mounts its ext2 root, reads /bin/busybox (2,858,424
bytes) out of it, and EXECUTES it —

    rustux: ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304 block=1024
            entries=6 walked=true etc/motd=19B
    rustux: ext2: ran /bin/busybox from the root true
    Rustux                      <- busybox's uname applet, run from the root

Two things had to be added: ext2.rs gained DOUBLE-INDIRECT reads (the
reader stopped at single-indirect, so a 2.8 MiB binary returned None),
and main.rs reads the inode's exact size into a kmalloc buffer and only
spawns when the read returned that many bytes (a short read would hand
the ELF loader a partial image).

So the installed system is not just a filesystem the kernel can read —
it is a system the kernel can run programs from.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: make it pid 1 (pivot), place the rest of the payload, then the
partition editor.

## run-247 — THE PIVOT: the installed system boots as pid 1

When the partition scan finds an ext2 root carrying /bin/busybox, the
kernel now boots THAT as pid 1 instead of the initrd payload:

    rustux: root: booting the installed system (pid 1 = sh from ext2)
    rustux: sched: launch sh (pid 1) rip=0x555500020b70

The initrd task loop is gated on `queued == 0`, so:
  - installed disk -> the root's binary is pid 1 (the initrd is skipped);
  - normal boot     -> unchanged (75 tasks, PASS, zero leak);
  - spawn failure   -> "falling back to the initrd" and the payload runs,
                       so a bad root can never leave the machine with no
                       init.

The binary is only remembered after the read returned the inode's exact
size, so a short read can't be booted.

That completes the arc: partition -> format ext2 -> place files -> mount
-> execute -> BOOT FROM IT.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak.
NEXT: place the fuller payload (29 MiB in the live initrd), give the
root an /etc and an init script, then the partition editor.

## run-248 — the payload grows, and a stack bug it exposed

Placing more files crashed the installer: `user fault #PF
task=livemenu rip=0x2` — a jump through a corrupted return address.
Cause: mke2_format held several 1 KiB scratch buffers on the stack
simultaneously across a recursive path helper, and each extra file
pushed the frame over the edge. The formatter is single-threaded, so
every scratch buffer is now `static` and stack use is constant.

With that fixed the payload list carries nested paths:

    bin/busybox
    lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libapt-pkg.so.6.0      (2007496 bytes)
    lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libapt-private.so.0.0  (424336 bytes)

VERIFIED: the install completes without a fault, e2fsck on the
installed root reports 0 issues, and debugfs lists both libraries at
their real paths.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.
NEXT: extend the list to the rest of the 29 MiB payload, add an init
script, then the partition editor.

## run-249 — two findings: FAT VBR mistaken for an MBR, and the real pivot gap

1. REGRESSION (fixed): the partition scan treated any sector ending in
   55AA as an MBR — but a FAT VBR does too. On the suite's superfloppy
   ESP it parsed garbage partition entries and did spurious I/O, which
   showed up as pgtest exit(5..8) on every boot while the suite still
   gated on exit 33 (exactly the "hidden failure behind a green suite"
   the repo rules warn about). The scan now skips sectors carrying a
   FAT BPB signature at offset 54 or 82. pgtest is exit(0) again on two
   consecutive runs.

2. THE PIVOT IS INCOMPLETE — and this is the honest headline. Spawning
   a binary from the ext2 root works, but the program's FILESYSTEM VIEW
   is still the initrd: path resolution goes through the initrd RAM fs,
   so /etc/inittab, /etc/init.d/rcS and /dev/tty* are not visible to it.
   Evidence: busybox `init` as pid 1 tries /dev/tty2..4 and exits 1
   even with an inittab present, because it cannot see the inittab.
   That is why the pivot uses `sh` for now.

   THE NEXT REAL RUNG is therefore a namespace pivot: make path
   resolution consult the mounted ext2 root (with /proc, /dev layered
   over it) instead of the initrd. Everything below that is proven —
   mount, read, execute — so this is a VFS change, not a filesystem one.

Suite PASS exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 212295 -> 212295 zero leak.

## run-250 — copy-up: the installed root's /etc becomes visible

Added user::root_copy_up(path, data): after mounting the ext2 root the
kernel copies its config files into the RAM fs, which is what an
initramfs boot does. Programs spawned from the root can now READ their
own /etc:

    rustux: root: copy-up etc/inittab true
    rustux: root: copy-up etc/motd true
    rustux: root: copy-up etc/rcS true

PROOF IT MATTERS: busybox `init` as pid 1 used to open /dev/tty2..4 and
exit 1 because it could not see /etc/inittab. With copy-up it reads the
inittab and prints "Please press Enter to activate this console" — i.e.
the installed system's own configuration is being honoured.

STILL BLOCKED: `can't run '/etc/rcS'` — exec has no #! (shebang)
support, so an installed init cannot run a shell script. That is the
next small rung; the pivot stays on `sh` until then.

Also fixed: root_copy_up stamped 0644, so a copied-up script was not
executable (EACCES); it now stamps 0755.

Suite PASS exit 33 twice, pgtest exit(0) both runs.

## run-251 — shebang support in exec (landed, but not the init blocker)

sys_execve now follows #! scripts: it parses the interpreter path
(capped at 127 bytes, stops at space/newline), re-resolves up to four
levels (so a script naming itself cannot recurse), and rewrites argv
Linux-style to [interp-basename, script, original argv[1..]] bounded by
MAX_EXEC_ARGS.

HONEST RESULT: this did NOT fix the installed init. busybox init still
reports

    can't run '/etc/rcS': No such file or directory

even though copy-up reported `copy-up etc/rcS true`, so the file is in
the RAM fs and executable (0755). The remaining cause is elsewhere in
exec's path resolution and is NOT identified — do not assume shebang
was the whole story.

Safe to keep: three consecutive suite runs PASS exit 33 with pgtest
exit(0), so the change does not regress anything.

State of the installed boot: `sh` from the ext2 root as pid 1 (works),
with its /etc copied up so it can read its own config. busybox `init`
gets as far as reading /etc/inittab (copy-up proven) but cannot run the
sysinit script.

## run-252 — exec's lookup never consulted the RAM fs (fixed); init still blocked

Root cause found for one part of the /etc/rcS mystery: `fs_lookup`
searched ONLY the initrd archive (`cpio::find`), so a copy-up file was
invisible to exec no matter how many times copy-up reported success.
It now checks the RAM fs vfiles first (canonical name, with a leading
'/' tolerated) before falling back to the archive.

That fix is correct in its own right and the suite is green
(pgtest exit(0), PASS exit 33) — but it did NOT unblock the installed
init: busybox still reports "can't run '/etc/rcS': No such file or
directory", and the execve trace showed the call arriving with
path=/etc/rcS. So at least one more defect sits between "execve is
called" and "the script runs"; it is NOT identified.

State: the pivot stays on `sh` from the ext2 root (works, with /etc
copied up). Do not assume the fs_lookup fix resolved the init path.

## run-253 — shebang interpreter must be link-resolved (landed, still not the init blocker)

A #! script's interpreter is often a link ("bin/sh" -> /bin/busybox).
The shebang path read the link's TARGET TEXT as if it were the image,
so the parse failed. It now runs the interpreter through resolve_links
first. Correct in its own right and the suite is green.

HONEST RESULT: still not the init blocker. With copy-up verified
(`copy-up etc/rcS true lookup=true`, i.e. fs_lookup DOES find it), the
interpreter link resolved, and execve traced arriving with
path=/etc/rcS, busybox still reports "can't run '/etc/rcS': No such
file or directory". So at least one further defect remains between
execve entry and script execution, and it is NOT identified.

Verified state: pivot stays on `sh` from the ext2 root (works, /etc
copied up); suite PASS exit 33 with pgtest exit(0).

## run-254 — init-script ENOENT: the key clue (no code change)

Every candidate was MEASURED, not guessed, and they all pass:

  copy-up etc/rcS           -> true
  fs_lookup("etc/rcS")      -> true
  resolve_links("etc/rcS")  -> Ok (resolve=0)
  canon_path("/etc/rcS")    -> correct (empty base for an absolute path)
  execve entry              -> traced, path=/etc/rcS
  probe inside the shebang block -> NEVER FIRES

THE CLUE: those same functions succeed when called from the KERNEL at
boot (the measurements above were taken there) but the task's execve
returns ENOENT and never reaches the shebang block. So the difference
is contextual, not algorithmic — something about the calling TASK's
state. Candidates for the next session, in order:

1. The task's fst()/cwd: canon_path reads fst(t) for a relative path.
   busybox passes an ABSOLUTE path, so this should not matter — unless
   copy_user_path truncated or mangled it.
2. copy_user_path's bounds: it copies from the task's address space;
   a short copy would produce a different string than the one traced.
   Log the LENGTH and the exact bytes copy_user_path returns, not just
   the rendered string.
3. The child that execs is a FORK of init — check that fork copies the
   fs state (cwd) and that the child's task slot is fully initialised
   before its first syscall.

Next probe: print copy_user_path's n and the raw bytes, plus fst(t)
cwd/cwdlen, at execve entry. That separates 1 and 2 in one boot.

## run-255 — init ENOENT: canonicalisation and resolution are both fine

The probe from run-254 ran. Results:

    dbg: canon=etc/rcS        <- canon_path is CORRECT
    (no "dbg: resolve FAILED" line)  <- resolve_links returned Ok

So the ENOENT cannot come from canonicalisation or link resolution.
Combined with the earlier table (copy-up true, fs_lookup true,
resolve_links Ok), the only remaining producer is the INTERPRETER
lookup inside the shebang handling: rcS starts "#!/bin/sh", so exec
looks for "/bin/sh", which in the archive is a LINK record to
/bin/busybox.

Tried: routing the interpreter through canon_path before resolve_links
(so the leading slash is stripped). Did NOT work — still "can't run
'/etc/rcS': No such file or directory" — so it was reverted rather than
left unverified.

NEXT PROBE (one boot): inside the shebang loop, print (a) the raw
interp bytes, (b) what canon_path returns for it, (c) what
resolve_links returns for that, (d) whether fs_lookup finds the final
name. One of those four is empty/wrong; the printout will say which.
Do not guess further before that trace — four hypotheses have already
been eliminated by measurement.

## run-256 — init ENOENT: it is the INTERPRETER (proven)

Final probe, with every earlier candidate already eliminated:

    dbg: canon=etc/rcS
    dbg: resolved=etc/rcS len=7 found=true

So the SCRIPT resolves and fs_lookup finds it. The shebang loop therefore
enters iteration 1 successfully (body starts "#!"), derives the
interpreter "/bin/sh", and fails on iteration 2 — where fs_lookup of the
interpreter returns None. That is the ENOENT, and it is the only
remaining producer.

Why the interpreter lookup fails: "/bin/sh" in the archive is a LINK
record whose data is the target text, not an ELF. resolve_links does
find the link, but the resolved target is "/bin/busybox" WITH a leading
slash, and the archive's names carry none — so the next fs_lookup misses.

NEXT FIX (small, one place): in the shebang loop, strip leading '/'
from whatever resolve_links returns before the next lookup (or call
canon_path on the resolved output). My earlier attempts stripped the
INPUT rather than the OUTPUT, which is why they did not help.

## run-257 — init ENOENT OBSERVED (not inferred): the interpreter arrives absolute

The loop-iteration trace settles it:

    dbg: it0 cur=etc/rcS shebang=true
    dbg: it1 MISS cur=/bin/sh

Iteration 0 finds the script and sees the shebang; iteration 1 misses
because `cur` is "/bin/sh" WITH the leading slash, and archive names
carry none. So the fix is exactly: strip leading '/' from the
interpreter before assigning `cur` (and before the resolve_links call
and its fallback — all three places must use the stripped slice).

My two earlier attempts failed because they stripped one place but the
fallback still assigned the unstripped `interp[..interp_len]`. The
current code path to edit is the `Err(_) =>` arm around line 1024 of
kernel/src/syscall.rs plus the `cur = &lname[..lname_len]` assignment.

State: tree clean at ba104c7, pivot on `sh`, suite green, both boot
paths PASS with zero leak. The fix is a five-line change with a
one-boot verification (look for "init script running" / "uname:").

## run-258 — the namespace pivot: the installed root copied into the RAM fs, pid 1 from ext2

The structural defect behind "installed system shows dev etc proc": fs_lookup
resolves vfiles + the initrd archive only — the mounted ext2 was never in a
process's namespace. The fix is NOT a VFS layer: the installed root (bounded to
512 files / 256 MiB) is copied whole into the RAM fs at mount time by
copy_root_dir (kernel/src/main.rs), which recurses INSIDE dir_walk's callback —
the Dirent is a stack copy and the PIO reads are sequential, so no live read is
re-entered. After that, fs_lookup/open/stat/getdents resolve the installed
files natively: ls / shows the installed tree, binaries exec out of it.

The pivot only fires on the installed disk: the installer ISO's initrd carries
hd.img, the installed ESP's initrd is the same archive minus hd.img, so
cpio::find(arc,"hd.img") gates both the copy-up and ROOT_EXE. Booting live
media with an installed disk still attached stays pristine and the installer
remains reachable. ROOT_EXE is fs_lookup("bin/busybox") taken after copy-up;
pid 1 = spawn(img,"sh"). set_initrd moved BEFORE the pivot so the initrd is a
read-only underlay — installed files win (vfiles resolve first), anything not
installed falls back to the archive.

Ordering fix that was masking everything on -machine pc: ata::init() ran AFTER
the partition scan, so the gate `ata::sectors()>0 || ahci::disks()>0` was
always false on i440fx (IDE only, no AHCI) and the scan never ran — neither the
install boot nor the installed boot ever mounted anything there. ata init is
now ahead of the scan.

livemenu: memset(files,0) — the uninitialized .read fn pointer was the
cr2=0xfffffffffffff8 installer crash; payload widened to 15 streamed entries
(busybox + sh/ash/ls/cat/cp/bash/dash/apt/dpkg/htop + ld-linux + libc + apt
libs); layout prompt emits its own newline and echoes the chosen root size.
user.rs: spawn sets PATH/HOME/TERM/USER — without PATH every bare command
reported "not found" even when the binary was right there.

Makefile run-iso: sendkey updated for the current menu (1 install -> Enter for
the default root size -> "yes" to confirm); INSTALLDISK 64->256 MiB (a 16 MiB
root cannot hold the ~35 MiB payload — mke2 FAILED -21 mid-format); install
wait 16->40 polls (formatting a 208 MiB root is minutes of one-block PIO
writes, not a hang).

Verified end-to-end: iso install PASS; eject; reboot;
`ext2: disk0p1 type=0x83 MOUNTED lba=98304`;
`root: 15 files copied up from ext2`; `pid 1 = sh from ext2`; `/ #` prompt;
`ls /` shows README bin dev etc lib lib64 livemenu proc sbin usr;
`uname -a` -> Rustux; `cat /etc/motd` -> the installed file.
make run: exit 33, 75 tasks, frames X -> X zero leak.

Audit run-258 scoped quick (security-audit skill): copy_root_dir bounds
(depth 8, 512 files, 256 MiB total, 32 MiB/file, name 96B, path 128B);
dir_walk already validates rec_len/name_len — CVE-2026-10645 class checked,
not vulnerable; superblock bounded by validate() — CVE-2026-13478 n/a;
hd.img gate is an exact cpio name match; spawn env writes are cap-bounded.
No findings. Recorded gap, not hidden: the installed boot lands on an
UNAUTHENTICATED root sh — busybox `login` still #GPs after its prompt
(/tmp/auth-work.patch holds the passwd/shadow payload + login pivot);
login-as-pid1 is the follow-up. Also open: two-external shell pipelines;
archive symlink records stat ENOENT in ls /bin output (cosmetic).

## run-259 — apt on the installed system: NSS/DNS, mke2 hardlinks + multi-block dirs, the multi-group allocator fix

Two reported defects: "apt missing folders" and "apt update cannot resolve
deb.debian.org". Both were data/layout problems, not kernel network code —
the UDP resolver surface (connect, sendto/recvfrom, sendmmsg, poll,
SO_ACCEPTCONN drop) was already complete.

DNS: glibc getaddrinfo needs the NSS chain — libnss_dns.so.2,
libnss_files.so.2, libresolv.so.2 were absent from the initrd. Added to
LIVE_PAIRS/LIVE_DEPS plus new etc/nsswitch.conf and etc/hosts. After that
`Get:1 http://deb.debian.org` resolves and fetches.

Missing folders: apt_dirs() only ran in live sessions; the installed boot
(busybox sh as pid 1) never seeded the apt/dpkg state tree. mke2 `.keep`
seeds now materialize var/lib/apt/lists{,/partial},
var/lib/dpkg/{info,updates,alternatives,parts,triggers},
var/cache/apt/archives{,/partial}, var/log/apt,
etc/apt/{sources.list.d,preferences.d,trusted.gpg.d}, tmp, root —
plus resolv.conf, nsswitch.conf, hosts, sources.list, 99rustux, and a
GENERATED var/lib/dpkg/status: the shipped-lib package table moved to file
scope and is shared between apt_dirs() and the seed, emitting real
Package:/Status: stanzas (the earlier "\n" seed parsed as an empty section —
"no Package: header" -> MergeList -> exit(100)).

mke2 upgrades: `alias` field = ext2 hardlink (dirent points at the target's
inode; no inode/blocks of its own) — ~54 busybox applet names plus
usr/bin/apt and the sbin applets now cost a dirent each, not a 2.8 MiB copy.
MKE2_MAX_DIRS 24->64, placed[16] -> MKE2_MAX_FILES(160). Multi-block
directories: mke2_dir_nblk() probes the packed span, root/subdir inodes get
nblk*BLOCK i_size and one direct pointer per block; the shared mke2_ent +
mke2_dir_write() emitter replaces the unbounded ents[2+16] into a 1 KiB
block (latent overflow — ~16 long names in one dir would have scribbled a
static buffer). i_links_count counts aliases; the inode bitmap marks only
non-alias files.

MAX_FDS exhaustion — why "32 files copied up" was really ~11: PAYLOAD opened
every streamed source before mke2_format and closed after, so open #12 hit
the 16-fd ceiling and every later entry silently skipped. file_reader now
takes the source PATH and opens+closes per block call; a payload of 150
streams never holds more than one fd.

copy_root_dir: dedup by inode — the ~55 dirents that share busybox's inode
copy once; the skipped names resolve through the initrd underlay's applet
symlinks, so /bin/rm still works without ~170 MiB of RAM copies.

Allocator correctness (found by audit, fixed same run): next_data_blk grew
contiguously THROUGH later groups' metadata — a ~45 MiB payload wrote file
data over group 1+'s backup super, GDT, bitmaps and inode tables (e2fsck:
"conflicts with critical metadata", garbage inodes 2049+, debugfs refused
the image outright). New data_range()/alloc_contig() hand out only blocks
inside each group's data span; dind+l2n allocate as one contiguous run so
the emit pass still finds level-2 blocks at dind+1+i. And the bitmap marking
itself was an OOB write: setbit(bm, blk-gs) on a 1 KiB group bitmap for
blk>=8192 scribbled neighbouring statics — mark_used() now offers every
allocated block to every group and only the owning group sets the bit, so
later groups' used blocks are claimed too.

Makefile run-iso third phase: after install+boot PASS it sends `apt update`
and gates on `apt-get exit(0)` (24x15s window; the 8.8 MB Packages fetch
takes ~3 min at ~57 kB/s).

Verified: iso install PASS; installed boot PASS (88 files copied up, pid 1
= sh from ext2); installed apt update PASS (Get:1 InRelease, Get:2 Packages
8790 kB, Reading package lists... Done, apt-get exit(0)); e2fsck -fn on the
extracted root: all 5 passes CLEAN (previously: metadata conflicts, garbage
inode tables, debugfs refused). make run: exit 33, 75 tasks, zero leak
(pgtest SA_SIGINFO FAILs = recorded open gap).

Audit run-259 scoped quick (security-audit skill): cve-now e2fsprogs parser
class (CVE-2022-1304, CVE-2019-5188, CVE-2019-5094, CVE-2015-0247,
CVE-2015-1572, CVE-2007-5497) — all crafted-image READER bugs; mke2 writes
a fixed installer table and the kernel reader bounds are audited (run-258).
Two findings inside the diff, both FIXED above: the setbit OOB write and
the metadata-overwriting allocator. needs_validation: none.
Known gaps: apt-key/keyrings absent -> Ign:1 tolerated by [trusted=yes];
copy-up is RAM-only (no persistence, ext2 stays read-only); installed boot
is still an unauthenticated root sh (/tmp/auth-work.patch); one transient
`sh: apt: not found` seen at t=0.5s post-boot, unreproduced on the same
image at t=11s — likely a tty-input race in the test loop, not the fs
(e2fsck clean, apt 2.8.4 runs from the identical disk).

## run-260 — apt install nano works: the `#!` shebang exec fix + update-alternatives

`apt install nano` reached "Setting up nano" then dpkg reported the
postinst "No such file or directory" while `ls` showed the file right
there and `sh nano.postinst` ran fine. Direct `#!` execve failed at the
interpreter: the shebang line yields "/bin/sh" WITH the leading slash,
but the flat namespace is canonical slashless — resolve_links scanned
"/bin", "/bin/sh" components that never match, returned it unchanged,
and fs_lookup("/bin/sh") missed every table (vfile_find/cpio::find are
exact-match) -> ENOENT. Every `#!/bin/...` script was broken: dpkg
postinsts, /etc/rcS-style boots, anything exec'd by path.
syscall.rs now strips the leading '/' from the interp name BEFORE
resolve_links so "bin/sh" resolves normally (cpio S_IFLNK -> target
text -> canon -> "bin/busybox"), and argv[0] comes from the interp
basename — not the resolved image — because busybox picks its applet
from argv[0] ("sh", not "busybox").

Second bug surfaced once the script actually ran: the rewritten argv
copied a shorter string over the caller's longer old arg and trusted
argv_len's +1 to be a NUL — the tail byte was the old arg's remnant
("sh"+"m" = "shm\0-less"), so busybox's strrchr(argv[0]) walked off
the highest stack string and faulted reading exactly stack_top
(cr2=0x555600100000, __strrchr_sse2 movups). argv[0]/argv[1] now get
explicit NUL writes after the copy (caps dropped to EXEC_STR_CAP-2).

Payload: /usr/bin/update-alternatives (real dpkg ELF, libc-only) is
now streamed + in initrd, var/lib/dpkg/alternatives/.keep seeds its
admin dir — the nano postinst's `update-alternatives --install` calls
succeed ("using /bin/nano to provide /usr/bin/editor|pico in auto
mode"). kernel/user/terminfo_l/linux ships the real linux terminfo so
TERM=linux binaries (htop) can open a terminal. run-iso gains a fourth
phase gating on `apt install nano` -> apt-get exit(0), and both apt
phases retype the command once when they see `apt: not found` or
exit(100) (the t=0.5s transient seen twice — still unexplained, but
now observed at every boot: stray `1\n` keys from the phase-2 wait
loop also land as `sh: 1: not found`).

Verified: headless disk boot -> apt update exit(0) -> apt install nano
-> "Setting up nano" -> update-alternatives x2 exit(0) -> dpkg exit(0)
-> apt-get exit(0) -> `nano --version` runs and exits 0. e2fsck -fn on
the extracted root: all 5 passes CLEAN (140 files). make run: exit 33,
75 tasks, frames 212293 -> 212293 zero leak (pgtest SA_SIGINFO FAILs =
recorded open gap; a pre-existing dpkg-deb #PF in-suite exits clean).

Audit run-260 scoped quick (security-audit skill): exec/shebang diff
reviewed — interp strip bounded (<=127 extract cap), argv rewrite
bounded (bn/sn <= EXEC_STR_CAP-2, explicit NUL, shift <=
MAX_EXEC_ARGS, shebang chain capped at 4); argv==NULL fallback
unchanged (CVE-2022-49264 class already enforced, CVE-2021-4034 is
the userspace consumer). livemenu additions are data rows under
MKE2_MAX_FILES. New binaries are trusted host copies. cve-now checked
the exec-argv + binfmt classes. No confirmed findings.
needs_validation: none.
Known gaps: apt-key/keyrings absent -> Ign:1 tolerated by
[trusted=yes]; copy-up RAM-only; unauthenticated root sh
(/tmp/auth-work.patch); transient `apt: not found` at ~0.5s post-boot
persists (retried around in run-iso) — unresolved, logged twice;
htop runs under TERM=linux now but console fidelity unverified;
enosys nr 435 (clone3) and nr 28 (madvise) logged during dpkg.

## run-261 — sh -> dash (dpkg applet shadow killed), perl-base installs, initrd integrity net

Bug the user hit: `apt install perl-base` (any package whose maintainer
scripts shell out to `dpkg` — nginx chain too) died with
`dpkg: unrecognized option '--validate-version'` printed in busybox
usage format. Root cause is BusyBox 1.36.1 SH_STANDALONE semantics in
ash.c: find_command/shellexec check find_applet_by_name BEFORE the
PATH search and run the applet via execve("/proc/self/exe",
["dpkg",...]); the PATH fallback only happens when that exec FAILS.
At the interactive prompt pid 1's /proc/self/exe -> "/sh" is a broken
link (non-canonical exec name) so the self-exec fails and the real
/usr/bin/dpkg wins — but every busybox CHILD (exec'd as bin/busybox)
has a valid /proc/self/exe, so inside maintainer scripts the busybox
dpkg applet shadows the real binary. Reproduced live: `sh -c 'dpkg
--version'` -> applet usage; `bash -c 'dpkg --version'` -> real
1.22.6; absolute path works everywhere. Debian never sees this
because it never points /bin/sh at busybox.

Fix mirrors Debian exactly: /bin/sh is now REAL dash on both live and
installed boots — livemenu ALIASES maps bin/sh -> bin/dash, LIVE_PAIRS
ships kernel/user/dash as bin/sh + bin/dash (previously dash only
existed in the test-suite initrd), LIVE_DEPS tracks it. dash needs
only libc + ld-linux (both shipped). run-iso gate "installed sh-dpkg
resolution" sends `sh -c 'dpkg --version | head -1'` and greps the
exec log for `usr/bin/dpkg exit(0)` (an applet run would print
proc/self/exe or bin/busybox). The phase-2 prompt gate moved from the
`/ #` grep to an `_RXOK` sentinel echo — dash's root prompt differs
from busybox's.

Second-order fallout: perl-base's preinst calls
dpkg-maintscript-helper (now REALLY running under usr/bin/dpkg) whose
line 594 calls `basename` — not shipped. Added the maintainer-script
applet set as busybox links on both live and installed trees:
basename dirname mktemp readlink cmp cut stat seq true false zcat tee
uniq printf (bin/) + env awk realpath getopt flock run-parts sha1sum
sha256sum (usr/bin/, matching Debian's canonical locations for things
scripts hardcode — /usr/bin/env being the famous one). gzip/gunzip
aliases were only in the installed table; now in LIVE_PAIRS too.

Verified in the GTK demo on a fresh install: apt update -> exit(0);
apt install -y perl-base -> Unpacking -> "Setting up perl-base" ->
dpkg exit(0) -> apt-get exit(0); `perl -e 'print 1+1'` -> 2. Kernel
exec log shows bin/basename exit(0) inside the maintainer script and
every dpkg call as usr/bin/dpkg. run-iso: all five phases PASS (92
files copied up).

Initrd integrity net (separate latent bug found mid-verification):
one GTK install produced a libapt-pkg.so.6.0 with a 71,680-byte zero
hole at file offset ~1.71 MB — exactly 35 x 2048-byte CD sectors,
2048-aligned in archive space — wiping apt's error-string table so
`E:` printed empty and everything exited 100. ISO on host is clean
(all 4 embedded copies), target.img install byte-perfect, initrd
file byte-perfect — the hole appeared only in that guest's RAM copy
of the archive, i.e. a transient firmware/El Torito read gap during
load_initrd (fresh QEMU RAM is zeros; a claimed-but-undelivered read
range stays zero). Detection, not root-cause: mkinitrd.py now emits a
rustux.manifest member ("name zlib-crc32" per regular file); livemenu
verify_payload() re-reads every member before touching the disk AND
again after mke2_format before writing the MBR — a mismatch aborts
the install loudly instead of writing a bricked disk; the kernel
prints `initrd crc32 = <zlib value>` at load (klib crc32_le is the
raw convention — !crc32_le(!0, arc) yields zlib, verified numeric
match against host zlib on "123456789" and on the full archive) so a
boot-time corruption is distinguishable from a mid-session RAM
scribble. Observed on this run's gates: pre+post verify "80 files, 0
bad" every cycle.

sendkey.py: QEMU sendkey has no uppercase names — str: now maps A-Z
to shift-<key> (was silently dropping capitals into the guest).

Audit run-261 scoped quick (security-audit skill): verify_payload
bounds reviewed (16KiB static manifest buf + NUL, name snprintf into
512B path, strtoul on hex, per-file 64KiB streamed reads, fds
opened/closed per entry under MAX_FDS, fail-closed !=0 abort);
manifest >16KiB would silently truncate coverage — ~6KiB today,
count printed makes truncation visible; manifest itself is
unverified (chicken-and-egg — trusted build artifact). klib crc print
is read-only over the bounded initrd slice. New applet links widen
the busybox surface — intentional, matches the Debian toolset
maintainer scripts expect. cve-now checked the cpio-extraction class
(CVE-2023-52138, CVE-2026-66484, CVE-2018-1263 et al — all
extract-time traversal bugs; n/a: our archive is build-trusted, never
extracted to disk, and the manifest is integrity detection not
signature verification). No confirmed findings. needs_validation:
the sector-drop root cause inside the firmware read path is still
unproven (detection shipped instead); run a few dozen ISO boots to
see whether `initrd crc32` ever disagrees with the manifest.
Known gaps: the manifest is unsigned — it catches read corruption,
not malicious media; methods/http still #PFs at the 1MiB stack guard
when run manually (cr2=0x5555ffffff70); pid 1's /proc/self/exe -> /sh
broken link persists (only affects applet self-exec, now moot).

## run-261b — initrd.crc sidecar: load-time verify + one retry

Follow-up to the integrity net: the archive now carries its expected
zlib crc32 as `initrd.crc` next to `initrd.cpio` on every ESP that
boots through load_initrd (hd.img's ESP for installed/live boots,
esp-iso.img for the installer ISO). load_initrd reads the sidecar,
crcs the archive as loaded, and on a mismatch re-opens the file and
re-reads the whole archive once before declaring it Bad — a
transient sector drop (the 35x2048B hole observed inside
libapt-pkg.so) now self-heals at boot instead of shipping a hole to
the installer verify. Boot log prints one of: `initrd crc32 = X
(verified vs initrd.crc)` / `initrd CORRUPT — crc X != initrd.crc Y
