(still bad after re-read)` / nothing (older media, no sidecar). This
also nails the (a)-vs-(b) question for any future corruption: a Bad
at t=0 means firmware/load; an Ok at t=0 with a later installer
verify failure means mid-session RAM scribble.

Verified: ISO boot prints `verified vs initrd.crc` and the at-use
recompute agrees; make run unaffected (suite boot has no sidecar —
NoSidecar path is silent).

Audit addendum: sidecar parse bounded (32B buf, utf8+hex parse,
from_str_radix); retry re-reads into the same bounded MAX_INITRD
buffer via the same read_all closure; a corrupt .crc read degrades
to NoSidecar, never a false Bad. The sidecar is a build-time
integrity tag, not a signature — it defends against read errors, not
malicious media. No findings.

## run-262 — apt install nginx end-to-end: TCP-orphan sweep (ENFILE), vtable capacity (ENOSPC), base-passwd, service stubs, getent, FIONBIO, faccessat2

nginx was a random apt test that kept surfacing real bugs; each got
fixed as it appeared. The chain now completes: `apt update` +
`apt install -y nginx` → `Setting up nginx` → `dpkg exit(0)` →
`nginx -v` prints nginx/1.22.1 on the installed system.

- ENFILE root cause was TCP slots, not OFDs. `fd_socket` returns
  ENFILE only when TCPSOCKS (MAX_TCPS=8) is full — OFD exhaustion is
  EMFILE. Orphaned FinWait/LastAck sockets (last fd closed before the
  peer's FIN+ACK) could only be freed by arriving peer segments; a
  lost FIN/ACK or a keep-alive server holding its side pinned a slot
  forever. apt's fetch loop opens+closes one socket per file → 8
  fetches = ENFILE. Fix: `tcp_retx` (the per-tick sweep) now also
  retransmits unacked FINs for FinWait/LastAck on the same doubling
  schedule (syn_at/syn_tries renamed ctl_at/ctl_tries, armed at every
  close transition in fd_shutdown + tcp_sock_close) and RST+frees
  orphans after a 12s deadline.
- ENOSPC mid-unpack was MAX_VFILES=1024/MAX_VDIRS=512 — perl-base
  alone is ~2700 files. Bumped to 8192/2048; the table-full paths now
  printk instead of failing silently.
- `chown: unknown user/group root:adm` — shipped the full Debian
  base-passwd set in kernel/user/etc/{passwd,group,shadow} (adm,
  www-data, _apt, etc.).
- `update-rc.d: not found` → stub script `stubs/noinit` (`exit 0`)
  installed as usr/sbin/update-rc.d and hardlinked to invoke-rc.d,
  deb-systemd-helper, deb-systemd-invoke; `stubs/locale` silences
  Debconf::Encoding.
- `getent: not found` broke passwd.postinst:7 (`getent group shadow`
  gid-42 check) → `stubs/getent`, a real flat-file lookup over
  /etc/{passwd,group,shadow,gshadow,hosts} (name → field 1, numeric
  → field 3, exit 2 on miss). Also satisfies nginx-common's
  `getent passwd www-data` guard so adduser is never invoked.
- Two install-path bugs surfaced by the stubs: MKE2_MAX_FILES=160
  truncated the ALIASES pass mid-table (awk/perl/deb-systemd-* never
  reached the ext2 — bumped to 320) and the stub sources needed +x.
- `nginx: [emerg] ioctl(FIONBIO) :80 failed (EINVAL)` — the ioctl arm
  predated OpenFile.nonblock; FIONBIO now toggles o.nonblock like
  fcntl(F_SETFL).
- faccessat2 (nr 439) routed to sys_access (the a4 flags are inert in
  the single-user model); kills the recurring `enosys: nr 439` spam.
- Exit telemetry kept: `pid N exe exit(code) ofds X tabs Y slots Z
  refs W tcps T` — it split the ENFILE misdiagnosis (clean OFDs) from
  the real TCP leak and costs one printk per exit.

Verified: GTK QEMU -machine pc, fresh ISO install (pre+post verify
83 files 0 bad), installed boot, `apt update` exit(0) then
`apt install -y nginx` exit(0) — tcps never exceeded 1; make run
exit 33, 75 tasks, frames X -> X zero leak.

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill — two in-diff findings
fixed (FIN retransmit advertised ack=0 instead of rcv_nxt;
tcp_sock_close's state write-back could resurrect a Closed state a
loopback-drained RST just set — now guarded by !k.dead). Verified
clean: stale bound, RTO0<<ctl_tries shift bound, FIN seq snd_nxt-1,
orphan gating (no fd/waiter can dangle), tick serialization vs IRQ.
needs_validation: post-loop tcp_free vs a future rx path that
enqueues mid-drain — unreachable with today's LO_Q semantics.
cve-now MCP unreachable this run (deviation recorded).
## run-263 — MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANONYMOUS shared memory: SHMSEGS segments, per-task VMA ledger, fork verbatim-share, phys-verified ownership

nginx's unconditional 1280B shm alloc at init forced the rung: mmap
deliberately ENODEV'd MAP_SHARED. Now implemented for the anonymous
case (file-backed shared stays honest ENODEV — no writeback exists).

- SHMSEGS: 16 global segments {frames[512], nframes, refs}. Task
  gains shms[(lo,hi,seg)] ×8 + nshms. shm_mmap allocates zeroed
  frames, maps via map_frame (caller-supplied-phys twin of
  map_page), adds range+VMA.
- fork_address_space aliases shm leaves verbatim (same frame) after
  shm_pin_all; fork failure path's shm_drop_all unwinds the pins.
  CLONE_VM children pin per copied VMA too; shm_mmap itself refuses
  under a shared mm (per-task ledger can't track two writers —
  ENODEV like file-backed, documented gap).
- The ownership test is shm_owns(t,va,phys) — phys ∈ ANY covering
  segment's frame list (OR over covers: MAP_FIXED can stack shm
  VMAs), never VMA coverage alone — after the audit found a
  range-based test lets a stale VMA (private MAP_FIXED remap,
  partial munmap) both shield private frames from freeing and alias
  them cross-task at fork.
- Unmap/exit paths: unmap_page and free_user_mappings skip only
  phys-verified seg frames; shm_unmap_range drops refs on fully
  covered VMAs at munmap, private/shm MAP_FIXED, brk shrink,
  nt_decommit/free_pages. shm_drop_all runs at task teardown AND
  unconditionally in reap (CLONE_VM non-last users leak refs
  otherwise — audit finding).
- tcp_retx hardened while in there: orphan deadline marks k.dead
  BEFORE the RST so a drain-side realloc can't resurrect the slot;
  the deferred free re-verifies dead+orphan; SynSent/FIN re-borrows
  check port-pair identity (slot reuse inside a loopback drain would
  otherwise bump a stranger's ctl_tries).

Verified: make run exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 211793 -> 211793 zero
leak; shmtest static-pie injected via initrd on the installed system
— 0 failures (maps, zeroed, child-write-visible-parent, private
stays private, munmap, multi-map); nginx gets past shm init
(FIONBIO + MAP_SHARED) — next blocker was /run/nginx.pid ENOENT
(mkdir fixes; not a kernel bug).

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-22 — three confirmed
findings fixed in-diff (rollback double-free; stale-VMA private-
frame aliasing via phys-verified shm_owns + shm_unmap_range at every
user-VA unmap; CLONE_VM non-last-exit ref leak via unconditional
reap shm_drop_all). Also fixed: aliased &mut SHMSEGS borrow in
shm_mmap; non-atomic MAP_FIXED nranges check moved pre-destroy.
needs_validation: tcp_retx slot-reuse mid-drain (defense added;
  a live realloc path unproven — residual: re-borrow checks lack
  peer_ip/state, schedule-corruption only); nt_view_unmap shm-ref
  coverage marginal (NT-only task issuing Linux mmap).
cve-now checked this run: CVE-2026-23127 (perf mmap refcount UAF —
class-match only, perf subsystem n/a); FIN_WAIT/socket-exhaustion
class query returned no Linux CVE match.

## run-264 — syscall-exit signal delivery restored (9c167cc regression), installed-boot auth + initrd robustness, run-iso 5/5 green

Found while chasing the POC's noisy failures: pgtest had been failing
6-10 checks nondeterministically for an unknown number of runs — the
suite only gates on qemu exit 33 so it never blocked anything. Blame
bisect (boots at a3721f4, f71544b, 73a2582 all red) + -L on the
dispatch tail pinned it: ia32-pipe2 commit 9c167cc dropped
`user::deliver_current(ctx)` from the x86_64 `handle_syscall` exit,
so self-directed kill() signals pended until the next scheduler pick
instead of delivering on the syscall's return — a race between the
user-mode check read and tick-driven delivery. The i386 handle_int80
path kept its call the whole time. Restored the one line; pgtest
went 9-failures -> 0, and the wild #PF reports turned out to be
pgtest's own intentional PROT_NONE guard-page SIGSEGV check working.

termtest's `FIONBIO -> EINVAL` check was stale: run-262 made FIONBIO a
generic OFD ioctl (Linux semantics — works on any fd). Check now
asserts set+clear succeed on the tty fd.

Installed-boot chain (landed with f955791, proven by run-iso 5/5):
pid 1 is `livemenu` (absent hd.img -> run_login -> passwd/shadow auth
-> bash), not a bare busybox sh — that was the auth regression;
copy_root_dir mints real vdirs for ext2 dirs via new
user::root_copy_dir (chmod/mkdir EROFS warnings gone);
load_initrd logs each failure stage (initrd_fail polling-UART note)
and retries open+read once — the warm-reboot "initrd: none found"
that silently killed livemenu->login (root cause of the whole sh-
fallback chain) now self-heals or at least names its stage. Makefile
run-iso: login gate sends root/rustux correctly, apt install -y,
install gate waits on `Setting up nano` not the stale apt-update
exit(0) line (was false-PASSing).

Verified: make run exit 33, 75 tasks, frames 211791 -> 211791 zero
leak, every test 0 failures (was pgtest 9 + termtest 1). run-iso 5/5:
iso install PASS, installed boot+live shell PASS (livemenu -> login ->
bash), dpkg 1.22.6 PASS, apt update PASS, apt install -y nano PASS
(`Setting up nano`, apt-get exit(0)); initrd verified vs initrd.crc
on the warm reboot. Remaining noise, root-caused by execve ENOENT tracing: apt forks
children that exit(127) for missing helpers — usr/bin/apt-key is
absent from the payload (apt's own W: names it; AllowUnauthenticated
covers it); the per-acquire http method exit(100) is the documented
non-fatal pending-error convention (Makefile INITRD_APT comment);
dpkg/tar PATH-probe ENOENTs are normal. None of it is a kernel bug —
every parent apt-get exits 0.

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-23 — diff is a
restoration of the audited call site + test check; cve-now
sigreturn/frame class checked (CVE-2017-1000255, CVE-2026-80560,
CVE-2019-13648, CVE-2026-46063 — privileged-frame-state classes,
inapplicable: UCtx carries user GPRs only). 0 findings.

## run-265 — ext3/ext4 read support + virtio-blk driver

ext-family reader (ext2.rs): incompat whitelist mount policy —
FILETYPE/RECOVER/EXTENTS/64BIT/MMP/FLEX_BG/EA_INODE/DIRDATA/CSUM_SEED/
LARGEDIR accepted; JOURNAL_DEV, META_BG, INLINE_DATA, ENCRYPT, bigalloc
refused rather than misread; RECOVER tolerated = same trade-off as
Linux norecovery ro-mount. Per-inode EXT4_EXTENTS_FL selects the map —
ext_walk walks the 60-byte iblock root then block nodes (depth<=5,
eh_entries clamped to node capacity, 48-bit starts, holes+unwritten ->
0). Classic map gained triple-indirect (iblock[14]) for huge ext3
files. Group descriptors honor s_desc_size + hi dwords under 64bit;
inode size_hi folded only for regular files. has_journal/uses_extents
drive the mount-line family name (ext2/ext3/ext4).

virtio.rs (new): transitional virtio-blk 1af4:1001 over the legacy I/O
interface — no MSI-X, no capability walk, polling like ata.rs. Probe:
io-BAR check, IO+busmaster, reset -> ACK|DRIVER -> features=0 ->
FEATOK -> queue PFN -> capacity -> DRVOK. Request: fixed descs 0-2
(header ctrl block, data block, status byte), avail bump, notify, spin
on used.idx. Two real bugs caught by the boot proof: (1) QEMU's
virtio-blk queue is 256-deep — laying the rings out at a 128 cap put
avail where QEMU reads zeros (all I/O silently stalled); the queue is
now allocated from the DEVICE's qsize. (2) snapshotting used.idx after
notify loses the race to a microsecond-fast completion — prev is read
before the kick. kalloc::alloc_frames(order) gives contiguous zeroed
DMA blocks; pio::inw added.

Proof: make run exit 33, 75 tasks, zero frame leak; ext3 and ext4 real
mke2fs images pass (ext4's sparse big.bin forces a depth-1 extent tree
— the recursive index->leaf path runs on Linux-written data); a
16MB MBR disk carrying the ext4 partition attached -drive if=virtio
mounts over the virtqueue (disk1p0 MOUNTED, 5 entries walked, hi.txt
copied up). esp.img bumped 64->96M — embedded test images + initrd
(57M) outgrew it. kbdtest 1 intermittent FAIL = pre-existing headless
input flake (stray CPR bytes, unrelated to diff).

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-24 — 0 confirmed;
nv-24-1 RECOVER tolerated recorded. cve-now: CVE-2026-34961
(eh_entries OOB — handled by capacity clamp), CVE-2025-38314
(used.len trust — n/a by design), CVE-2026-99682/-2026-80808/
-2025-38222 n/a. Known limits (documented, next-rung candidates):
first virtio device only, transitional transport only (modern-only
1042 needs the cap-list interface), /dev/vdN not minted in the OFD
block layer, no journal replay, no metadata checksum verify, virtio-net
not written.

---

## run-266 — virtio-net datapath (RX/TX over the shared Vq)

virtio.rs refactored: the split-ring machinery (setup, put_desc,
avail_push, notify, used_idx/entry, poll_once) extracted into a shared
`Vq` type used by both blk and net. net_init probes transitional
virtio-net-pci (1af4:1000) on the same legacy I/O interface — RX/TX
queues at device qsize, 16 pre-posted 2KiB device-writable RX buffers,
8 TX slots, 10-byte legacy net header with ZERO negotiated features
(no mergeg_bufs/GSO — the device cannot split frames). net_rx polls
used-ring via wrapping_sub vs our own rx_seen; id bounded <N_RX; len
>=NET_HDR required; copy clamped to slot capacity AND caller buf.
net_tx pads to 60B, single-descriptor. net_selftest ARP-requests the
slirp gateway and drains RX — proven: `virtio: net ARP round-trip
true` alongside the e1000e's own round-trip.

REGRESSION CAUGHT: naming any `-netdev` suppresses QEMU's implicit
default NIC — the whole socket suite had silently been riding it
(udptest's sendto->EHOSTUNREACH e6, tcptest exit(1), wiotest flake).
Restored explicitly: `-netdev user,id=n0 -device e1000e,netdev=n0`
beside virtio-net on n1. Makefile run now names both NICs; sockets
still gate on e1000::net_up only (virtio-net is NOT wired into the
socket layer — net_rx/net_tx are driver-level; the NIC-abstraction
that would let user.rs pick a NIC is the documented next step).

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-25 — 0 confirmed;
in-run hardening (rx len clamp lacked the slot-cap term). cve-now:
CVE-2025-40292 (host len>buffer overread — clamped), CVE-2025-38551
(ANNOUNCE probe race — n/a, no config irqs), CVE-2010-0741 (TSO
whitelist — n/a, zero features), CVE-2025-38314 class (used.len —
never consumed). Verified: make run exit 33, 75 tasks, frames equal,
udptest/tcptest/nettest/wgettest exit(0), ext4-over-virtio mount ok.

Known limits (documented, next-rung candidates): virtio-net not wired
into sockets (net_up/net_mac/net_rx/net_tx ready; the socket layer's
NIC abstraction is the next step — then route egress by NIC and demux
ingress from both rings), no IRQ-driven rx (polling only), virtio
modern-only transport (cap-list) absent, NVMe absent, /dev/vdN absent.

---

## run-267 — virtio-net wired into the socket layer (NIC abstraction)

e1000.rs is now the NIC-agnostic IP stack; virtio-net is a second NIC
beneath it. tx_eth is the single egress switch (e1000 live ring when
programmed, virtio otherwise — patching src MAC and the ARP body's
sender-sha to the egress NIC so slirp's replies come back where the
device filters them in). virtio_rx_one mirrors drain_desc on the
virtqueue: answers who-has-us ARP out the ARRIVAL NIC, reports op2
replies for arp_resolve's spin, feeds IPv4 to ip_demux. rx_drain
(runs every tick + before socket blocks) now drains both. net_up()
is NIC-agnostic. A virtio-only boot seeds the identity statically
(seed_virtio_identity: 10.0.2.15/24, gw 10.0.2.2 — QEMU's fixed
slirp layout; no-op once any identity exists). route_mac ARP-resolves
the gateway when GW_MAC is uncached; both drain paths cache it.

Three real bugs caught by the boot proofs: (1) net_rx ate a posted
buffer per malformed used-entry — silent RX starvation; in-range ids
now re-post. (2) N_RX=16 was thin under 75-task load — now 64. (3)
off-link routes had no path to an uncached gateway MAC — the first
external connect timed out on virtio-only; route_mac falls back to
arp_resolve(GW_IP) now.

Proof: `make run-vnet` (virtio-net the ONLY NIC) — exit 33, zero
leak, DNS resolve + TCP connect :80 + HTTP/1.1 200 OK, busybox wget
6152 bytes off deb.debian.org, all socket tests exit(0). Dual-NIC
`make run` unchanged: e1000 preferred, all green.

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-26 — 0 confirmed.
cve-now: CVE-2025-40292 re-verified (clamp + repost); ARP-spoof
class = solicited-replies-only window, narrower than Linux.

Still missing (next-rung candidates): virtio IRQ-driven RX (polling
only — works because rx_drain runs every tick, but it's latency not
interrupts), modern-only virtio transport (1042 cap-list), NVMe,
/dev/vdN in the OFD layer, per-NIC ARP caches + routing table if a
second real segment ever exists.

---

## run-268 — NVMe storage driver

nvme.rs: QEMU's nvme function (1b36:0010; 0004 fallback), 64-bit BAR0
MMIO, the spec dance — CC=0/RDY, admin SQ16+CQ16 via AQA/ASQ/ACQ,
CC.EN|RDY, identify controller (NN) + namespace 1 (NSZE, 512B LBAF
only), I/O CQ32+SQ32 created with the ring's phys in PRP1, NVM read
commands (PRP1=data, BATCH=8), completions polled by the CQ phase
bit and tagged by cid. DiskIo's 4th transport; the suite's shared
MBR+ext4 image mounts over BOTH virtio and NVMe.

Three spec bugs the proofs caught: (1) completion DW3 decoded as
{status,cid} — it's {command_id low16, status high16} with phase at
bit16. (2) create-CQ/SQ need the ring phys in PRP1 — without it QEMU
binds queues at phys 0 and the datapath silently dies (sector0
selftest caught it). (3) QEMU refuses qprio=0 — its priority range
is 1-based.

Read-only datapath (no write command yet); single namespace; no
MSI-X (phase polling); no SGL/PRP-list (PRP1 covers ≤4KiB).

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-27 — 0 confirmed.
cve-now: CVE-2023-6238 (device-DMA class — copy-out bounded by
request); nvme-tcp + teardown classes n/a. make run exit 33, zero
leak, ext4 disk2p0 MOUNTED walked=true.

Next candidates: NVMe write_range + /dev/nvme0n1 in the OFD layer,
modern virtio transport, virtio IRQ RX.

## run-269 — NVMe write path + write datapath proofs

nvme.rs gains write_range: OP_WRITE (0x01) NVM command, the audited
read path's mirror — same index/bounds gates, BATCH-capped copy-in
to the staging page, PRP1 carries the page, cid-tagged phase-bit
polling. Both drivers now selftest writes at lba 64 — MBR gap before
the ext4 partition; -snapshot keeps the host image clean anyway.
Read -> write pattern -> verify -> restore -> re-verify, all true
on virtio and NVMe. virtio::write_range exercised for the first time
(was dead API — clippy warning cleared).

Audit: scoped quick, security-audit skill, run-28 — 0 confirmed.
cve-now: nvme-tcp target classes + bhyve hypervisor classes + keep-
alive teardown all n/a; CVE-2023-6238 device-DMA class extends to
writes via the same staging-bounded copy-in. make run exit 33,
zero leak, both ext4 transports still mount.

Next candidates: /dev block nodes in the OFD layer (write_range has
no userspace reach yet), virtio IRQ-driven RX, modern-only virtio
transport (cap list), multi-namespace NVMe.

## run-270 — /dev block nodes + pwrite64 + NVMe doorbell fix

user.rs grows the transport-generic block layer: blk_capacity,
blk_transport, blk_read/blk_write (bounce-buffered byte interface —
the same contract sd_read/sd_write have). Nodes: /dev/vd[abcd]
(major 254, minor 16*i) for probed virtio disks, /dev/nvme0n[1-4]
(major 259, minor=ns) for NVMe namespaces — hardware-gated minting
like sd. Open gate ENXIOs dead minors; lseek SEEK_END through
blk_capacity; BLKGETSIZE/BLKSSZGET/BLKGETSIZE64 trio added.
Two ABI fixes surfaced by the compiled proof: fstat on a Dev OFD
reported S_IFCHR for every node (now the stored S_IFMT), and
pwrite64 was ENOSYS — implemented for Ram + block devs, plus a
block arm in fd_pread.

Real driver bug found by the proof, not review: the NVMe SQ tail
doorbell carried the monotonic counter — the spec doorbell is a
ring INDEX and QEMU rejects writes >= qsize, so the I/O queue
wedged permanently at submission 32 (right after the init mount).
Every later nvme op timed out (sf=0xdead). Fix: doorbell writes
tail % size. CQ head was already index-bounded.

blktest.c: 16 checks — open/fstat majors, SEEK_END cap, all three
BLK ioctls, pread sector0 55AA, unaligned edge splice, pwrite/
verify/restore roundtrip at lba 64 on BOTH /dev/vda and
/dev/nvme0n1, mknod(254,16) -> ENXIO, BLKGETSIZE64 on /dev/null ->
ENOTTY. 0 failures; suite 76 tasks, exit 33, zero leak.

Audit: scoped quick, run-29 — 0 confirmed. cve-now: pread/pwrite
+ BLKGETSIZE classes — no entries.

Next candidates: /dev partition minors (vda1/nvme0n1p1 from the
MBR scan), virtio IRQ-driven RX, modern-only virtio transport,
multi-namespace NVMe.
## run-271 — /dev partition minors (sda1..4, vda1..4, nvme0n1pN)

The MBR scan now registers every validated partition into a
DEV_PARTS table (dev_part_add — part 1-15, lba/sectors u32-clamped)
via ext2::transport (pub) mapping the DiskIo index to (major,
drive). blk_window resolves an rdev to (transport, drive,
base_lba, cap_bytes): minor%16==0 is the whole disk, 1-15 looks
up the registered partition and re-validates base+cap<=disk_cap
per call. blk_read/blk_write run at base+lba. The nvme minor
scheme moved to Linux's drv*16+part (was ns-per-minor) so all
three majors share one encoding; the sd/ahci read/write arms are
consolidated onto blk_* (sd_read/sd_write/sd_drive/ahci_read/
ahci_write deleted); the open gate is blk_capacity>0 for all.
dev_init mints sda1..4 / vda1..4 / nvme0n1p1..4 for registered
partitions only, after the whole-disk nodes.

blktest grew +10 checks (26 total): vda1 + nvme0n1p1 open, rdev
minor decode, BLKGETSIZE64 = partition size (not disk), pread of
the ext4 magic at +1080 inside the window, last-sector read at
the window end, crafted mknod vda5 -> ENXIO. 0 failures over
both transports.

iotest's IN_CREATE check failed once under suite load — rerun
green; it's a watch-queue race (any task touching / queues
events ahead of inochild.tmp), not this diff.

Audit run-30: 0 confirmed — CVE-2026-64318's class (on-disk
count driving an OOB partition scan) is handled by design:
fixed 4-slot MBR, u32 bounds at registration, per-call
re-validation in blk_window. Commit pushed origin main.
## run-272 — virtio-net IRQ-driven RX (MSI-X on the legacy interface)

The virtio NIC now raises MSI-X vector 0xf4 on RX used-buffer
completions: probe_io returns the function bdf, NBDF stores it,
net_irq_selftest arms MSI-X table entry 0 -> IRQ_VNET -> this
LAPIC (rest masked), binds the RX queue to entry 0 via the legacy
R_MSIX_Q register (config-change and TX stay at MSIX_NO_VEC —
only RX fires), and proofs it with the same ARP probe + hlt-wait
e1000 uses: "net 1 MSI IRQs on vec 0xf4 (rx-caused), reply
received". apic.rs gains IRQ_VNET + a dispatch arm; cpu.rs the
stub + IDT gate.

net_on_irq only reads R_ISR (the architected ack) and counts —
never drains. rx_seen/avail mutations are unguarded, so an
IRQ-context drain could re-enter an interrupted syscall-side
net_rx and double-post a descriptor. The IRQ's real value is
waking the sti;hlt idle loops: a blocked socket task reschedules
and drains at arrival latency instead of the next 100Hz tick.
Ordering note: MSI-X enable shifts the legacy device config
window +4 — the MAC is read during net_init while MSI-X is off.

Audit run-31: 0 confirmed — CVE-2021-47613's partial-completion
class handled (consumers re-check the ring; IRQ is only a wake).
Commit pushed origin main.
## run-273 — modern virtio transport (non-transitional virtio-net)

virtio.rs grows a second transport alongside the legacy I/O
interface: virtio_caps walks the vendor cap list (id 0x09)
collecting common/notify(+mult)/isr/device windows as physmap VAs
(bounds hardened mid-run — vendor caps only parsed at off<=0xe8,
bar index rejected >=6, chain capped 32); handshake_modern does
reset-poll + ACK|DRIVER + requires/accepts VIRTIO_F_VERSION_1 +
FEATOK; Vq::setup_modern publishes desc/avail/used phys
separately via common_cfg, reads per-queue notify_off, enables;
Vq carries notify_va/isr_va so notify() and the ISR ack branch
per-transport; VNet gains hdr (10 legacy / 12 v1 header) and
common (modern cfg VA, also selects where the MSI-X vectors are
written). net_init tries modern first, legacy fallback intact.

Real bug caught by the boot proof: the v1 header is 12 bytes and
the TX copy still ran at offset 10 — bytes 10-11 (num_buffers)
read garbage MAC bytes and the wire frame lost its first two
bytes; slirp dropped everything until the copy moved to n.hdr.
Also fixed: the IRQ selftest's first wake can deliver a
stale/noise frame — the reply-wait now drains a bounded window.

Makefile flips the net device to virtio-net-pci-non-transitional
on both run and run-vnet (blk stays transitional — both paths
exercised per boot). Proven end-to-end: modern mac probe, ARP
round-trip, MSI IRQ 0xf4 reply received, and run-vnet's full
socket suite (nettest + wgettest) over the modern-only NIC.

Audit run-32: 0 confirmed — cap-walk bounds hardening applied
in-run (CVE-2026-13216 class). Commit pushed origin main.
## run-274 — multi-namespace NVMe

nvme.rs binds up to MAXNS(4) namespaces: identify-ctrl's NN is
clamped to the table size and drives a per-ns identify loop —
each ns must pass nsze!=0 AND lbads==512 to bind (inactive ns
identify as zeroed and are skipped, logged). NSID[] records the
real nsid per bound index so a skipped ns can't silently renumber
later disks; read/write commands carry the validated nsid and
bound against NS_SECTORS[i]. The sector0 + write-verify-restore
proofs now run per bound disk.

Makefile: nvme,id=nvme0 + nvme-ns nsid=1/2 (QEMU 8.2 accepts the
auto-attached nvme-bus). Boot proof: ns1+ns2 bound (ns3/4 zero,
skipped), disk0/disk1 sector0+write proofs green, ext4 mounts
over disk2p0 AND disk3p0. blktest +5 checks: /dev/nvme0n2 open,
S_IFBLK 259:16, BLKGETSIZE64, pread 55AA, pwrite/restore — 31
checks, 0 failures.

Audit run-33: 0 confirmed — CVE-2026-89971's failed-identify
class handled (zero data never binds). Commit pushed origin main.

## run-275 — GPT partition-table support

The disk scan was MBR-only; real disks use GPT. gpt_scan in main.rs:
protective-MBR detect (any slot's type 0xEE) -> LBA-1 header parse —
"EFI PART" sig, header size 92..=512, header CRC over the size-limited
region with the crc field zeroed -> entries array bounded inside the
disk (elba*512 + nent*esz, checked mul+add, nent<=4096, esz 128..=1024
mult-of-8, <=512KiB region) -> usable-range sanity (first_usable!=0,
last_usable>=first_usable, inside disk) -> single-pass chunked stream
that accumulates the entries CRC AND parses each fully-contained entry
(esz<=512 entries always land whole inside a sector chunk) -> entries
must sit inside the usable range AND the disk -> on CRC mismatch or
mid-stream IO failure parts[] is zeroed before return. Entries fill
parts[] by GPT index so sparse tables keep pN numbering; only indices
1..=15 fit the minor nibble. The unified parts list feeds dev_part_add
+ ext2::mount unchanged — GPT and MBR disks coexist.

Two bugs found mid-run: BlockIo::read takes a BYTE offset, not an lba
index (read(io,1) hit byte 1 of the MBR -> all-zero header); and the
header-CRC check compared raw-vs-stored-final (always-reject).

tools/mkgpt.py builds the test image: protective MBR, primary+backup
GPT headers with real zlib CRC32s, one Linux-fs GUID entry holding
ext4_test.img at lba 2048. Makefile wires it as nvme-ns nsid=3 ->
nvme disk2 -> /dev/nvme0n3 (259:32) + /dev/nvme0n3p1 (259:33).

Boot proof: `gpt: 1 partition(s) registered` -> `disk4p0 MOUNTED`
type=0xfc63daf (the GUID prefix — ext4 walked, motd read). blktest
+9 checks: nvme0n3 protective-MBR pread (55AA + 0xEE at 446+4),
nvme0n3p1 open/S_IFBLK 259:33/BLKGETSIZE64/ext4 magic at +1080/
last-sector pread, unregistered p2 minor -> ENXIO — 40 checks,
0 failures. Exit 33, 76 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-34: 0 confirmed — CVE-2011-1577 (crafted header size) and
CVE-2011-1776 (unchecked entry size) handled by the clamps +
fixed-size buffers; CVE-2026-64318 count-parse class handled.
Ledger at 194 CVEs. Commits pushed origin main.

## run-276 — virtio-rng entropy feed + pool reseed

The pool was a one-shot rdrand seed at boot with no reseed path —
and no virtio-rng, the canonical guest entropy tap (QEMU feeds it
from the host's getrandom). virtio.rs gains a third device: probe
transitional 0x1005 through the shared legacy path, post 8x64B
DESC_WRITE fill buffers, SPIN-bounded drain with an absolute
RNG_SEEN cursor, id<N_RNG + len.min(64) gates, drained slots
re-posted. rand.rs gains reseed(): entropy XOR-folds into the
ChaCha key words + rdtsc into the nonce — future output stays a
function of every seed ever fed (a weak source perturbs, can't
wipe); counter bumps and idx=64 drops the stale block buffer so
no old-key bytes are drawn again. Empty input is a no-op.

Proof: `rng 0x60e0 fed 256B -> reseed (nonuniform true)`;
/dev/urandom nonzero ok. Exit 33, 76 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-35: 0 confirmed — CVE-2023-53998's writer/reader race
n/a (boot-context only), CVE-2026-87043's unready-device class
handled (pool seeded first; absent device returns 0, reseed
no-ops). Ledger at 196 CVEs. Commits pushed origin main.

## run-277 — NVMe MSI-X I/O-CQ interrupts

The NVMe datapath only spin-polled the CQ phase bit — no interrupt
delivery at all. nvme.rs now arms MSI-X before Create-IOCQ (the
CQ's interrupt vector is fixed in cdw11 at create: ien+iv=1 when
armed), masks every entry then routes entry 1 -> IRQ_NVME (0xf5);
the admin CQ's architected IV0 stays masked. on_irq is count-only —
the phase-bit poll stays the sole completion consumer, same
contract as virtio-net's handler. irq_selftest runs post-sti: one
read must grow the IRQ count (bounded wait).

BUG found by the proof: pci::msix_cap dropped the 64-bit BAR's
high dword — NVMe's BAR at 0xc00000c000 truncated the MSI-X table
address into low memory and every entry write was silently eaten
(msix=true, 0 IRQs). The cap now reconstructs the high dword when
type bits read 0b100 — fixes the class for every caller.

apic.rs IRQ_NVME + dispatch arm; cpu.rs stub + IDT gate 28;
main.rs calls irq_selftest in the post-sti block.

Proof: `nvme: 2 I/O-CQ MSI IRQs on vec 0xf5 (read-caused)`,
`I/O-CQ MSI receive path true`. Exit 33, 76 tasks, zero leak,
blktest 0 failures.

Audit run-36: 0 confirmed — the MSI-X lifecycle CVEs
(CVE-2026-46141, CVE-2026-80620) are teardown/domain classes, n/a
with no unload path. Ledger at 198 CVEs. Commits pushed origin
main.

## run-278 — virtio-console /dev/hvc0

virtio.rs gains the virtio-serial console channel. QEMU wiring:
`-chardev socket,path=/tmp/hvc.sock,server=on,wait=off,id=vc0
-device virtio-serial-pci -device virtconsole,chardev=vc0` —
`virtio-console` is NOT a QEMU device name; the console is a
virtconsole PORT on a virtio-serial bus. tools/hvcfeed.py is the
socket peer: injects `rx-proof-line` every 250ms, captures guest
TX to /tmp/hvc_captured.log.

Probe: transitional DEV_CON 0x1003, handshake_feat accepts only
F_MULTIPORT (bit 3). The multiport topology took empirical
decoding — QEMU builds one ivq/ovq pair per EXISTING port in id
order, then the control pair (identifiable by qnum 32 vs data
queues' 128), then spare pairs. The 'queue interleave at
2i/2i+1, control at 2*(max_ports+1)' spec reading was wrong:
with one port QEMU creates only 6 queues; control is sel 2/3.

Control protocol: ports start CLOSED under multiport. Post ctrl
event buffers, send DEVICE_READY {id 0, ev 0, val 1}, device
answers PORT_ADD on the ctrl-in queue carrying the port id
(0 — collided with the first sentinel, now u32::MAX), send
PORT_OPEN {id, ev 6, val 1}, then set up the port's data pair
(sel 2*id / 2*id+1) and post 8x128B RX buffers.

Data path: con_drain consumes used entries — id<N_CON gate,
len.min(128).min(FIFO room) before copy into a 2KiB kernel FIFO,
re-post + notify on ANY consumption (a zero-length completion
used to re-post silently). console_read drains+copies;
console_write stages one desc + poll_once; console_pending
drains for the poll path.

user.rs: /dev/hvc0 minted rdev 229<<8 only when console_present;
open gate `229 if console_present => i, 229 => ENXIO` (the
missing arm was the first open FAIL — fell through to ENODEV);
read arm 0xe500 range_ok_w -> console_read; write arm ->
console_write; poll arm POLLIN while bytes queue.

Bugs the run dug out: (1) port-id sentinel 0 == QEMU's real id
0; (2) open gate had no 229 arm; (3) drain re-post without
notify on zero-len completions.

Proof: `console ctrl ev=1 id=0` -> `console 0x60c0 rxq=128
txq=128 tx-proof 28B`; host capture shows both the init line and
`hvc-user-tx-proof`. hvctest 8 checks 0 failures — open, fstat,
S_IFCHR, rdev 229:0, write, poll POLLIN, rx bytes,
rx-proof-line. Exit 33, 77 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-37: 0 confirmed — used-ring id/len trust and
port-id/queue-sel trust bounded by design; CVE-2025-38064 /
CVE-2022-49100 n/a. Commits pushed origin main.

## run-279 — virtio-input evdev events

virtio.rs gains virtio-input. GOTCHA: QEMU's q35
`virtio-keyboard-pci` resolves to the NON-transitional variant
(1af4:1052 — MMIO cap-list, no legacy I/O BAR), unlike
virtio-serial-pci which stayed transitional. The first probe on
the legacy id (0x1012) found nothing; the driver now tries the
modern path (virtio_caps + handshake_modern + Vq::setup_modern
from run-273) and falls back to legacy — both converge on the
same transport-agnostic Vq.

Queues: sel 0 = eventq (device fills 8B virtio_input_event
{type,code,value} into posted buffers), sel 1 = statusq
(guest->device LED reports, unused). vinput_drain: used id<N_VIN
gate, len ignored (fixed-offset parse), EV_KEY only, val<=2,
push via kbd::ev_push_ext into the shared evdev ring — merged
with the PS/2 stream exactly like Linux feeding multiple
keyboards into evdev. kbd::ev_len drains the queue first, which
reaches every consumer (read arm, evdev_wake inside stdin_pump,
poll arm) — all syscall/idle context; on_irq never calls ev_len.
New pairing to know: EVR now has IRQ-side (PS/2) AND
syscall-side (virtio) producers — writes stay index-bounded,
loss-only, same as the ring's existing drop policy.

Proof chain: tools/kbdfeed.py drives QMP send-key 'shift' every
300ms (shift = evdev records, zero tty bytes — can't pollute
console readers). QEMU drops taps sent before the guest posts
eventq buffers, so the feed runs 18s. Kernel-side transport
proof: VIN_EVENTS counts drained EV_KEY on the virtio queue —
the PS/2 path cannot fill it. `virtio: input '' evq=64` (name
read comes back empty from this QEMU — cosmetic) -> `input 5
evdev event(s) through virtio-input` -> `receive path true`.
vintest: 9 checks, 0 failures — open/fstat/S_IFCHR/rdev 13:64/
short-read EINVAL/poll POLLIN/press+release. Exit 33, 78 tasks,
zero leak.

Audit run-38: 0 confirmed — device-field/used-ring/config-size
trust all bounded; teardown CVEs n/a. Ledger at 202 CVEs.
Commits pushed origin main.

## run-280 — virtio-net control queue

handshake_modern is now handshake_modern_feat(common, accept_lo):
accepts the subset of requested low-dword features the device
offers (VERSION_1 still required). net_init_modern negotiates
F_NET_CTRL_VQ (bit 17) — QEMU's non-transitional virtio-net
offers it by default. Control queue = sel 2 (queue pairs first,
ctrl last per spec); binds only when the feature negotiated AND
C_NQ >= 3.

net_ctrl(class, cmd, data): {class,cmd}+data as an OUT desc
chained DESC_NEXT to a 1-byte device-writable ack desc — the
only thing the device may write. poll_once waits the chain;
ack==VIRTIO_NET_OK(0) is success.

Selftest runs at init (poll-based, pre-sti): CTRL_RX promisc +
allmulti (class 0, cmd 0/1) and CTRL_MAC_TABLE_SET (class 1,
cmd 0). BUG the proof caught: MAC table layout is
{nuni, uni_macs[], nmulti, multi_macs[]} — the first build put
nmulti before the unicast table and QEMU acked error; fixed by
ordering the unicast set first.

`net ctrlq promisc true allmulti true mac-table true` —
three device-acked commands. ARP round-trip and the MSI IRQ
proof unaffected. Exit 33, 78 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-39: 0 confirmed — staging bounds, feature-masking,
queue-count gate all by design; CVE-2022-48864 (vdpa device-side
validation) n/a. Ledger at 203 CVEs. Commits pushed origin main.

## run-281 — virtio-balloon driver (run-40 audit, pushed)

Transitional virtio-balloon (DEV_BALLOON 0x1002 — QEMU numbers
transitional devices by slot: rng was 0x1005/subsys 4, balloon is
0x1002/subsys 5, no conflict). Legacy handshake, inflate/deflate
queue pair (sel 0/1), zero feature bits.

The pump rides stdin_pump — which runs in tick IRQ AND fd_read
syscall context. Three defects the run dug out:

1. Re-entrancy — an IRQ pump preempting a syscall pump could
   race the held-PFN registry and double-post deflate desc 0.
   BAL_BUSY compare_exchange guard: re-entrant calls skip.
2. Publish order — BAL_IO (the pump's readiness gate) was stored
   before BAL_IV/BAL_DV; an IRQ in the window let the pump
   allocate held pages with no queue to post (bounded leak).
   BAL_IO stores last now.
3. Deflate protocol inversion — the deflate vq carries
   guest-posted OUT PFNs, not a device-filled IN buffer. The
   first build posted a standing DESC_WRITE buffer that could
   never complete and read completion PFN counts off `len`
   (QEMU leaves it 0 for OUT). Now the pump copies held PFNs to
   the staging half-page, posts OUT, and frees only
   held_remove-verified PFNs after the used completion.

And a test-harness bug: balloonfeed's fixed sleeps sent
`balloon 1024` before the guest's first config read — QEMU
latches num_pages, so the deflate erased the inflate request
before the pump polled it (6367 guest config reads all saw 0,
confirmed via `-trace virtio_balloon*`). The feeder now holds
each target and polls query-balloon until the guest's `actual`
confirms the phase.

Host-side proof: `balloon 1008` -> guest inflated 4096 pages ->
QMP actual=1056964608; `balloon 1024` -> deflate freed 256-page
chunks back to kalloc -> actual=1073741824. Exit 33, 78 tasks,
`frames 207797 -> 207797` zero leak.

Audit run-40: 0 confirmed — target bounded by BAL_MAX,
held_remove verification, kalloc IRQ-safety all by design;
CVE-2026-74481 (suspend-time UAF) n/a. Ledger at 204 CVEs.

## run-282 — block write-cache flush (run-41 audit, pushed)

The write-capable block layer had no durability path at all —
writes completed when the command retired, not when data hit
the medium. Flush commands added on every transport:

- virtio-blk: negotiate F_BLK_FLUSH (bit 9, fallback to plain
  handshake), REQ_FLUSH = 2-desc chain (hdr OUT + status IN)
- NVMe: opcode 0x00 on the I/O SQ per-ns — no PRP
- AHCI: FLUSH CACHE EXT (0xea) non-data — count=1 keeps issue()'s
  PRDT a valid 512B descriptor (count=0 underflows to 4MiB)
- PIIX ata.rs already flushed per write; standalone flush_d added

User surface: blk_flush(rdev) dispatches like blk_rd/blk_wr
(partition minors flush the parent disk); BLKFLSBUF (0x1261)
with the size-trio's node gating; fd_fsync drives the flush on
block fds only — RAM/archive fds keep the honest-0 answer.

Proof: `flush=true` negotiated + REQ_FLUSH acked on vda; nvme
flush true on all 3 namespaces; ahci disk0 flush true; blktest
+8 checks 0 failures (BLKFLSBUF, fsync, fdatasync, ENOTTY).
Exit 33, 78 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-41: 0 confirmed — unsupported-op gating by design
(CVE-2024-47680 pattern), PRDT bound, fsync scope. One fix:
flush_d's index guard compared against a sector count.
CVE-2023-54008 (vdpa affinity) n/a. Ledger at 206 CVEs.

## run-283 — block discard/TRIM (run-42 audit, pushed)

The storage command set was missing discard — mkfs.ext4 issues
BLKDISCARD and every transport path answered ENOTTY. Now:

- virtio-blk: F_BLK_DISCARD (bit 13) negotiated in the same
  masked pass as F_FLUSH; REQ_DISCARD carries
  {sector,num_sectors,flags} segment tables (1024-sector
  segments, <=128 per command) through the data staging block
- NVMe: DSM opcode 0x09 with the 16B range table via PRP1,
  cdw10=nr-1, cdw11 deallocate bit
- ATA/AHCI: no TRIM wired -> EOPNOTSUPP (correct Linux answer
  for a device without discard)
- user.rs: blk_discard dispatch (Option<bool> — unsupported vs
  command-failure split), BLKDISCARD/BLKSECDISCARD arms with
  the size-trio gating + alignment + window bounds

Bug the review caught: the ioctl arm first bounded the
node-relative start against base+cap — right bound is cap
alone (partition byte 0 = partition base), in subtraction form
so start+len can't overflow past the check.

Proof: `discard=true` negotiated; BLKDISCARD acked on vda +
nvme0n1; BLKSECDISCARD ok; EINVAL on misaligned/len0;
EOPNOTSUPP on /dev/sda; ENOTTY on /dev/null. blktest 55 checks
0 failures. Exit 33, 78 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-42: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 206 CVEs.

## run-284 — virtio-scsi storage transport

Fourth block transport: QEMU `virtio-scsi-pci` (1af4:1004
transitional, legacy I/O BAR) + `scsi-hd` on the same
$(VIRTIO_DISK) image. Driver lives in virtio.rs: `scsi_cmd` issues
one request = cmd_req 51B header (8B flat LUN `{1,target,
0x40|hi,lo,...}`, u64 tag, task_attr/prio/crn, 32B CDB slot) OUT,
optional dataout OUT, resp IN, optional datain IN — descriptor
chain must put every OUT before every IN and terminate the last.
`S_RESP` is 128B because QEMU requires the resp iovec to cover
`sizeof(resp)+sense_size` (10+96); a short chain hits
`virtio_scsi_bad_req` -> `virtio_error` -> `vdev->broken` and the
device never completes another kick (debugged via
x-query-virtio-queue-status: broken=true, last-avail-idx stuck).
LUN field is at header offset 0, not 1.

Disks: `SDisk{vq,ctrl,data,sectors,lun}` in SDISKS[2], bound on
TUR+INQUIRY+READ CAPACITY(10); scsi_read/write batch S_BATCH=8
sectors through an 8KiB staging block; scsi_flush = SYNCHRONIZE
CACHE(10). Init does the standard datapath selftest (sector0 sig,
write/verify/restore at LBA64, flush).

Block layer: SCSI disks ride Linux's extended sd major **65**
(minor 16*drv), so partition minors and dev_part_add work like
vd/nvme — ext2::transport index 4 after nvme, DiskIo len/read
arms, main.rs scan tmajor `_ => 65`, six `8 | 254 | 259` dispatch
sites widened to `8 | 65 | 254 | 259`, partition-name match gets
a 65 arm (letters continue past ahci -> sdb/sdb1).

Proof: `virtio: scsi inquiry 'QEMU' 'QEMU HARDDISK' last_lba
32767`, `scsi write datapath true verify true restore true flush
true`, `ext4: disk5p0 MOUNTED` (through transport-4 reads),
blktest +10 checks incl. sdb/sdb1 open/fstat 65:0/65:1/
BLKGETSIZE64/MBR sig/ext4 magic/pwrite-verify-restore. Exit 33,
78 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-43: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 208 CVEs.

## run-285 — SG_IO scsi generic pass-through

The sg(4) ABI on top of run-284's transport: `/dev/sgN` char nodes
(major 21, one minor per probed scsi disk, minted in dev_init) +
`SG_IO` (0x2285) accepted on sg nodes *and* whole-disk scsi block
nodes (major 65, minor%16==0 — partition nodes are rejected, see
below). The ioctl arm parses the 88-byte `sg_io_hdr_t` (x86_64
field offsets incl. the 8-alignment pad before usr_ptr@56),
validates the header whitelists, then hands the CDB to
`virtio::scsi_passthrough` which issues it through one request
chain and returns raw status/response + bounded sense — the
caller sees CHECK CONDITION as status=2 + sense like Linux, not
an errno.

Gates (three were security findings fixed in-run):
- dxfer_len <= 4096 (the disk's S_BATCH staging bound) checked
  before pointer validation — oversize is EINVAL not EFAULT.
- FROM_DEV zeroes the staging first: a no-data-phase CDB (TUR
  with dxfer_len>0) must return zeros, not prior IO contents —
  the CVE-2018-1000204 uninitialized-dxfer class.
- Partition nodes (minor%16!=0) reject SG_IO — a partition fd
  would issue commands across the whole disk, the CVE-2011-4127
  bypass class.
- TO_DEV direction on an O_RDONLY fd is EACCES — write commands
  can't bypass the open mode.
- Documented divergence: no CAP_SYS_RAWIO opcode whitelist
  (CVE-2012-4542 needs a capability model we don't have).

Proof: sgtest 24 checks 0 failures — hdr size, sg0 open/fstat
21:0, INQUIRY vendor 'QEMU', READ CAPACITY last_lba 32767, TUR,
SG_IO on /dev/sdb + READ(10) MBR sig, EINVAL/EFAULT/ENOTTY/
EACCES gates. Exit 33, 79 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-44: 0 confirmed, 3 fixed in-run. Ledger at 214 CVEs.

## run-286 — NVMe pass-through ioctls

The nvme-cli ABI: `/dev/nvme0` controller char node (major 243)
plus `NVME_IOCTL_ADMIN_CMD` (0x41B5) and `NVME_IOCTL_IO_CMD`
(0x41B7), reusing run-285's gate model — whole-disk block nodes
or the controller node only, partition nodes ENOTTY, a writable
fd stands in for CAP_SYS_ADMIN (the opcode-whitelist divergence
is documented; no caps model). nvme.rs `passthrough` stages the
*caller's* buffer into SCRATCH/DATA before issuing (so write-
direction commands work and a short device write can never
return stale kernel data — the pre-fill is the user's own
bytes), PRP1 is force-pointed at staging (no user phys addr),
and the completion status field becomes the ioctl's positive
return value like modern Linux. IO_CMD forces nsid from the
block node; on the controller node it's ENOTTY.

Proof: nvmtest 21 checks 0 failures — IDENTIFY ctrl (vid 0x1b36,
serial nv0), IDENTIFY ns (nsze 32768), ADMIN_CMD on nvme0n1,
IO_CMD READ lba0 -> MBR sig, ENOTTY/EACCES/EINVAL/EFAULT gates.
Exit 33, 80 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-45: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 216 CVEs.

## run-287 — loop devices

The losetup ABI: `/dev/loop-control` (misc 10:237) with
`LOOP_CTL_GET_FREE`, `/dev/loop0-7` block nodes on major 7 with
direct minor indexing (no partition nibble), and the LOOP_*
ioctl family — SET_FD/CLR_FD/SET_STATUS64/GET_STATUS64/
SET_CAPACITY. A `Loop` binds an fd's backing — `FdKind::File`
initrd slices (read-only) or `FdKind::Ram` VFiles (read-write),
bounded by `lo_offset`+`lo_sizelimit` — and the loop rides the
normal blk dispatch (pread/pwrite/BLKGETSIZE64/fsync all work).

Two semantics caught in-run:
- Unbound loop minors must *open* — losetup needs the fd for
  SET_FD. The gate is `7 => i`; transport answers ENXIO on reads
  and CLR on unbound.
- `Ram` backings pin the backing OFD — without it the VFile slot
  could be freed+reused while bound and the loop would alias an
  unrelated file's bytes.

Proof: blktest +24 checks, 0 failures — GET_FREE, unbound open/
ENXIO, SET_FD + EBUSY, capacity, read/write cross-path verify,
STATUS64 offset/limit, CLR_FD, rebind, EINVAL/ENOTTY gates.
Exit 33, 80 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-46: 0 confirmed, 2 fixed in-run. Ledger at 220 CVEs.

## run-288 — CDROM ioctl family via AHCI ATAPI

`/dev/sr0` grows real CD-ROM ioctls. Key discovery: on q35 the
`media=cdrom` drive is an **AHCI ATAPI device on SATA port 2**,
not the legacy 0x170 channel (OVMF shows `Sata(0x2) DVD-ROM`).
So the transport lives in ahci.rs: `SIG_ATAPI` (0xeb140101)
recorded during the port scan, `ATAPI_MEM` lazy scratch with a
4KiB buffer, `issue_packet` — PACKET (0xa0) H2D FIS with the
byte count in LBA mid/high, the CDB in the ACMD area, PRDT only
for data commands — sharing the disk path's stop/program/start
sequence.

Ioctls on the sr node: `GET_CAPABILITY` (fixed honest mask),
`LOCKDOOR` (arg-as-value PREVENT ALLOW — Linux takes the flag
by value, not pointer), `READTOCHDR`/`READTOCENTRY` via READ
TOC (descriptor walk double-bounded), `READMODE1` (msf at
arg+0 -> lba -> ATAPI READ(10) 2048B into the same buffer).

QEMU: `mkisofs` generates the test ISO at build time, attached
`if=ide,index=2,media=cdrom`.

Proof: blktest +11 sr0 checks, 0 failures — PVD `CD001` read
back through READMODE1. Exit 33, 80 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-47: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 226 CVEs.

## run-289 — AF_PACKET capture sockets

`socket(AF_PACKET, SOCK_RAW|SOCK_DGRAM, htons(proto))` — the
tcpdump/dhcpcd ABI. `PKTSOCKS[4]` each hold an 8-deep ring of
`PktFrame` (wire frame + sockaddr_ll metadata); `pkt_tap` runs at
every link boundary — `drain_desc`, `virtio_rx_one`, `lo_drain`
(inbound) and `tx_eth` (outgoing -> PACKET_OUTGOING). Filters are
the socket/bind protocol (host-order ethertype, ETH_P_ALL wild)
and the bound `sll_ifindex` (0=any, 1=lo, 2=nic). Cooked
(SOCK_DGRAM) strips the 14-byte header on recv and composes it
from the sockaddr_ll on sendto — src mac forced, no spoof.

recvfrom answers a fully-initialised `sockaddr_ll`; a blocked
receiver gets the frame through its own tables like udp_rx.
MAX_TASKS went 80->88 — pkttest was the 81st spawned record.

Two in-run findings: the IRQ/syscall re-entrancy on the capture
rings (PKT_TAP_BUSY guard — nested tap drops, never corrupts)
and the run-288 LEAK regression — `ATAPI_MEM`'s lazy alloc
landed 5 frames past the baseline; now allocated in the init
port scan like the disk `MEM[]` slots (zero leak restored).

Proof: pkttest 24 checks 0 failures. Exit 33, 81 tasks, zero
leak.

Audit run-48: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 231 CVEs.

## run-290 — AF_UNIX sockets + socketpair

`socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_STREAM|SOCK_DGRAM)` + `socketpair(2)` (NR 53
was absent — wired through the syscall table). `UNIXSOCKS` holds
endpoints, `UNIXCONNS` connected pairs (two 4KiB byte rings,
open[]/rd_shut[] per side), `UNIXDQ` the flat datagram queues
(si*4 slice — kept off the enum for clippy's large-variant rule).

sockaddr_un honours both namespaces: paths stop at the first NUL,
`\0`-abstract names span the addrlen tail — addrlen can be 3, so
the `len>=8` bind/connect prologues yield to the unix arm's own
gate. Stream connect() enqueues on the listener backlog and
answers 0 (Linux completes before accept); accept() mints the
server fd. A full backlog blocks on UnixConn(li,ui) — the waker
performs the enqueue since block_switch never returns for a
retry. Datagrams resolve bound names (or the connect/socketpair
peer) into per-sock queues; queue-full is honest loss.

Three in-run findings: name-collision matched the bind caller's
own name (re-bind EINVAL ordering), blocked connects resumed
with stale rax (waker now completes them), and wait arming left
stale wait_deadline values. All fixed before commit.

Proof: unixtest 49 checks 0 failures — duplex streams via
socketpair and via bind/listen/connect/accept, shutdown
EOF/EPIPE, both name kinds, FIONREAD, poll, recvfrom source
name, a fork()'d cross-task read. Exit 33, 82 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-49: 0 confirmed, 3 fixed in-run. Ledger at 239 CVEs.

## run-291 — recvmsg (NR 47) + SCM_RIGHTS fd-passing over AF_UNIX

recvmsg was entirely absent (caught by a `=>0` stub group); sendmsg
parsed `control`/`controllen` and ignored them. Now: scm_parse walks
the cmsg list for SOL_SOCKET/SCM_RIGHTS, refs each OFD (+1) into a
UnixFds batch, and scm_emit mints them as fresh fds in the RECEIVER's
fdt (MSG_CMSG_CLOEXEC honoured). Stream rights position-attach —
FdAttach{at,fds} lands on the peer's consume list at the send's
first-byte ring position and delivers with the read that crosses it;
dgram rights ride the UDgram row. Blocked recvmsg arms wait_ptr=
msghdr VA; the UnixDgramM/UnixRecvM wakers re-parse under the
sleeper's pml4 and deliver fully (scatter + name + cmsg + flags).
A blocked sendmsg parks its gather in KSEND[conn][side] kernel
staging — the UnixSendK waker pushes it since the caller's stack
VA is gone.

Two in-run findings: scm_parse's `fdt.fds[fd]` used the user fd
unbounded — fd>=16 panicked the kernel (now .get -> EBADF); and
every post-parse error path held +1 OFD refs forever (bail! macro
releases at all of them, plus queue-full/conn-dead/plain-recv/
full-attach drops). No unix GC needed — refs are explicit, queue
death releases them.

Proof: unixtest 70 checks 0 failures — fd pass over stream +
dgram socketpairs (the received fd really works), cmsg shape,
MSG_CTRUNC drop+flag, two-iovec scatter, blocked recvmsg waker
delivers rights cross-task. Exit 33, 82 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-50: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 243 CVEs.

## run-292 — AF_NETLINK + NETLINK_ROUTE

socket(AF_NETLINK=16, SOCK_RAW|SOCK_DGRAM, proto=0) — the family
was entirely absent, so `busybox ip` and glibc getifaddrs (wget's
resolver path) had nothing to talk to. NLSOCKS[8] holds endpoint
state {pid, groups, raw}; a flat NlGram queue per sock carries
reply blobs. bind() auto-assigns pid = task pid with a collision
scan -> EADDRINUSE; getsockname answers sockaddr_nl. Requests
arrive via write/send/sendto/sendmsg (all staged through one
bounded parser): nlmsg walk requires len >= 16 and off+len in
bounds; RTM_GETLINK/GETADDR/GETROUTE build fixed-shape dumps
(lo + eth0, 127/8 + 10.0.2.15/24, link-subnet + default-gw +
loopback routes) followed by NLMSG_DONE, all queued as one blob;
unknown types answer NLMSG_ERROR(-EOPNOTSUPP). recv/recvfrom/
recvmsg deliver name+payload (+iovec scatter + msg_flags for
recvmsg); empty queues park on NlRecv/NlRecvM with the waker
delivering through the sleeper's tables. sendmsg's MSG_DONTWAIT,
poll POLLIN and FIONREAD all honour the queue.

Three in-run findings: sock_name wrote 8 bytes into getsockname's
4-byte socklen_t* -> OOB kernel->user stack write (the source of
the mysterious `ip` hang — it clobbered busybox's rtnl_handle);
reply nlmsg_pid was stamped 0 instead of the destination port id
— busybox rtnl_listen and glibc getifaddrs filter replies by
portid and skipped the whole dump -> blocked recvmsg forever
(wget hung the same way); and RTA_GATEWAY went out at attr type 2
(RTA_SRC) instead of 5.

Proof: nltest 33 checks 0 failures, including real-binary
`busybox ip link|addr|route` all parsing our dumps end to end.
Exit 33, 83 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-51: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 249 CVEs.

## run-293 — AF_UNIX SOCK_SEQPACKET + recvmmsg

socketpair/socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_SEQPACKET) — the last socket-type
hole. The seq flag rides UnixSock (fresh→listen→accept→connect
propagation) and UnixConn (ring framing): records go into the
same two byte rings as [u16 len][payload]. Every send path is
atomic — inline VA, inline slice, and both blocked wakers —
free < 2+len blocks rather than half-write (EMSGSIZE past
ring-2); KSEND staging grew to 4098 so a framed record fits.
Recv paths consume exactly one record: surplus past the caller
buffer discards, recvmsg reports MSG_TRUNC, FIONREAD answers
the pending record's payload len, SO_TYPE answers 5. A seq
client can't reach a stream listener (ECONNREFUSED) and vice
versa. recvmmsg (NR 299) is the recv twin of sendmmsg —
per-entry recvmsg with msg_len writeback, short-stop on the
first error after >=1.

Two in-run hardenings: the seq send waker wrote the record
header before copying the payload — a bad sleeper VA mid-copy
committed a half record (now tail only advances on success);
and rl is clamped to avail before `head += 2 + rl` — a corrupt
record length can't desync the ring into stale reads.

Proof: unixtest +19 checks, 0 failures — record boundaries,
truncation discard, MSG_TRUNC, FIONREAD, EMSGSIZE, type
mismatch refusal, recvmmsg batch + drained EAGAIN. Exit 33,
83 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-52: 0 confirmed, 2 hardened in-run. Ledger at 249 CVEs.

## run-294 — /dev/net/tun (TUN/TAP virtual packet fds)

open("/dev/net/tun") (char 10:200) mints a private FdKind::Tun per
open over TUNS[4]; TUNSETIFF attaches (IFF_TUN/IFF_TAP, IFF_NO_PI),
TUNGETIFF reports, TUNSETNOCSUM a noop. Slot i owns the fixed
10.200.i.0/24 subnet (no SIOCSIFADDR — fixed per slot); tx_eth
detours dst-matching IPv4 into the tun's read queue — TUN strips
wire padding to ip total_len, TAP queues the whole frame.
write() injects inbound: TUN payloads feed ip_demux, TAP frames
cross the capture tap + eth ingress. TunRecv parks a reader;
tun_push delivers through the sleeper's tables. FIONREAD = next
record len incl. PI; poll shows POLLIN/POLLOUT.

Two in-run findings: tun_route indexed the subnet byte as f[33]
(the host octet — 10.200.0.9 resolved ti=9 and fell through to the
wire; it's f[32]); and tun_alloc could leak a slot when the three
post-mint bail paths (ENOTDIR/OFD-EMFILE/fd-EMFILE) returned —
each now releases it (4 failures would have exhausted the table).
Also fixed a test-side trap: getsockname on a UDP socket reports
0.0.0.0 (no stored local addr) — injection dst is the hardcoded
slirp IP like pkttest's gw.

Proof: tuntest 33 checks, 0 failures — pre-setiff EIO, flag
EINVAL, EBUSY re-attach, outbound capture (no_pi + PI header),
inbound UDP injection seen by a bound socket both modes, fork'd
blocked-reader wake, reopen freshness. Exit 33, 84 tasks,
zero leak.

Audit run-53: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 249 CVEs.

## run-295 — memfd_create (NR 319) + file sealing

memfd_create mints an anonymous O_RDWR file: a Ram-backed VFile
born unlinked so no path ever names it, reclaimed by
vfile_free_if_dead at last close (dup/fork/SCM_RIGHTS views hold
real OFD refs). The name is a display label — <=249 bytes like
Linux, stored "memfd:<label>" so fd links read
"/memfd:name (deleted)" (the link printer now appends " (deleted)"
for any unlinked-while-open inode, matching Linux's format).
Flags are whitelisted to MFD_CLOEXEC|MFD_ALLOW_SEALING —
HUGETLB/EXEC bits get an honest EINVAL since no hugetlbfs or
exec-scope machinery exists.

Sealing: VFile.seals holds the F_SEAL_* set on the inode (shared
by every aliased view, which is the point of the ABI). Without
MFD_ALLOW_SEALING the file is born F_SEAL_SEAL; fcntl(1033/1034)
accept memfd fds only, EINVAL non-memfd and unknown bits, EPERM
once SEAL_SEAL is set. Enforcement covers all three remaining
size/content mutators: write/pwrite EPERM under SEAL_WRITE or
SEAL_FUTURE_WRITE, extension past EOF EPERM under SEAL_GROW
(in-bounds overwrite stays legal), ftruncate EPERMs per direction
under GROW/SHRINK. path-truncate can't reach a born-unlinked
inode by construction.

No in-run findings — the two structural n/a's worth recording:
CVE-2026-63952 (SEAL_EXEC's implied SEAL_WRITE lands after the
writable-map check) can't exist here — SEAL_EXEC is rejected at
the fcntl mask, MFD_EXEC at mint, and file-backed MAP_SHARED mmap
is ENODEV outright. And CVE-2026-99336/-64283/-99473 (KVM
guest_memfd), CVE-2024-50182 (memfd_secret), the gup/hugetlb
memfd families — separate syscalls, all unimplemented.

Proof: memfdtest 46 checks, 0 failures — mint/flags/name bounds,
O_RDWR/S_IFREG/link-shape/path-invisibility, the seal fcntl gate
set, GROW/SHRINK/WRITE/SEAL_SEAL enforcement across write/
pwrite/ftruncate, dup+fork+SCM_RIGHTS shared-seal proofs,
MAP_PRIVATE on a fully sealed file. Exit 33, 85 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-54: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 255 CVEs.

## run-296 — pidfd family (open 434 / send_signal 424 / getfd 438)

`FdKind::Pidfd(pid)` — an anon-fd process handle. next_pid() is
monotone so a stored pid can never alias a recycled task: the
whole pid-reuse pinning problem Linux solves with struct pid is
structurally absent. open gates flags!=0 / pid<=0 EINVAL (pid
read as i32 — rdi upper bits are user garbage, pid_t is 32-bit),
missing pid ESRCH. send_signal EINVALs flags!=0 or info!=0
(Linux requires info==NULL), EBADFs non-pidfd fds, ESRCHs dead
targets, then rides kill()/pend_one — sig 0 is the liveness
probe. getfd EINVALs flags, EBADFs targetfd >= MAX_FDS or an
empty slot, ESRCHs dead targets; it clones the target's OFD —
refs+=1, rolled back on EMFILE, never CLOEXEC — the same shared-
object semantics SCM_RIGHTS delivers (seals/position/socket
state ride along). Documented divergence: no ptrace_may_access
gate — no creds model exists and kill() is just as open.
poll POLLIN iff the target is dead; wake_pollers() in
exit_bookkeeping fires blocked pollers when a task dies
(proof: poll(-1) wakes on child exit). No io surface:
read/write EINVAL, lseek ESPIPE, anon-inode stat,
"anon_inode:[pidfd]" link.

Two in-run fixes, one subtle. (1) The suite task cap turned out
to be OFD-bound not task-bound (85 tasks x 3 std streams = 255
vs MAX_OFDS 256) — but raising MAX_OFDS to 512 exposed the real
bug: FdTab.fds stored Option<u8>, so OFD index >=256 truncated
and aliased OFD 0. pidfdtest (86th spawn) got bogus std
streams: writes vanished, syscall answers came from the wrong
OFD, the pause() child never died — a silent cross-fd confusion
worse than the ENFILE it was hiding. Widened FdTab.fds,
EpollEnt.ofd and ofd_revents' param to u16; MAX_TASKS 96
alongside. (2) My syscall constants were off — send_signal is
424 (435 is clone3) and getfd is 438 (436 is close_range);
verified against asm/unistd_64.h. Every pfd_sig/pfd_getfd call
had been hitting ENOSYS.

Proof: pidfdtest 0 failures — all gates, SIGTERM kill + waitpid
reap, dead POLLIN/ESRCH, blocked-poll wake on exit, and the
getfd fd-clone proof via a child writing through a pipe the
parent pulled out of its table. Exit 33, 86 tasks, zero leak.

Audit run-55: 0 confirmed, 2 fixed in-run. Ledger at 258 CVEs.

## run-297 — clone3 (435) + close_range (436) + epoll_pwait2 (441)

sys_clone3 parses the 88-byte clone_args the way Linux does:
size < 64 EINVALs, > PAGE_SIZE E2BIGs, and bytes past struct v2
must all be zero (E2BIG — a caller can't smuggle fields through
the tail). Signal bits may NOT live in flags (EINVAL — they
belong in exit_signal, which merges into the merged-flags low
byte; valid-but-undeliverable signals still ENOSYS via clone's
own gate). ptid/ctid map onto PARENT_SETTID and
CHILD_SETTID|CHILD_CLEARTID; set_tid/cgroup EINVAL — set_tid
would let a caller pick pids and break next_pid's monotonicity
(the invariant that makes FdKind::Pidfd safe). CLONE_PIDFD is
the headline: it EINVALs with PARENT_SETTID, pre-flights the
*pidfd pointer and OFD/fd room BEFORE spawn (Linux allocates
the pidfd file inside copy_process — an EMFILE must not leave a
zombie without a handle), then mints FdKind::Pidfd(child) —
composing directly with run-296's machinery, proven by pidfd
SIGKILL + POLLIN on the clone3'd child.

fd_close_range EINVALs first>last, unknown flags, and the
UNSHARE|CLOEXEC combo (Linux: meaningless); last clamps to
MAX_FDS-1, empty slots pass silently. UNSHARE fdtab_dup's a
private table (every OFD ref bumped) before releasing the old
index — proven by a CLONE_FILES thread that detaches then
closes a range while the parent's fd survives. CLOEXEC marks
the range. fd_epoll_pwait2 takes a timespec: NULL waits
forever, bad sec/nsec EINVALs, sub-ms rounds up to the next ms.

Three in-run fixes: CLONE_PIDFD leaked into the merged flags
(tripped clone's ENOSYS on every PIDFD call); the clone_args
field check was off by one (cgroup was aimed at a[8]=set_tid —
real indices a[8]/a[9]/a[10]); and stack+stack_size was an
unchecked u64 add — a user-controlled overflow panic, now
checked_add -> EINVAL.

Proof: cl3test 41 checks, 0 failures. Exit 33, 87 tasks, zero
leak — zero FAILs suite-wide this run.

Audit run-56: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 259 CVEs.
## run-298 — openat2 (437) + process_mrelease (448)

openat2 wires the full RESOLVE_* discipline: sys_openat2 validates the
open_how (size<24 EINVAL, >4096 E2BIG, nonzero tail E2BIG, single
range_ok'd copy — the CVE-2022-48832 audit-deref class structurally
absent); fd_openat2's canon_beneath scopes '..' to the dirfd floor
(BENEATH EXDEVs escapes and absolute paths; IN_ROOT clamps and treats
dirfd as root). Audit finding: the literal-component scope alone left a
symlink inside the subtree free to escape — resolved path must now keep
the base as a component prefix or EXDEV (CVE-2026-77763 class, both abs
and rel targets refused). NO_SYMLINKS ELOOPs on any rewrite or final
link; NO_MAGICLINKS ELOOPs on /proc fd links. process_mrelease gates
flags/fd/live/reaped/shared-mm like Linux, then releases a zombie's mm
early — pml4 user-half cleared so reap's re-walk can't double-free.
Audit run-57: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. oa2test 38 checks green;
exit 33, 88 tasks, zero leak. Ledger at 263 CVEs.
## run-299 — futex_waitv (449) + fchmodat2 (452)

futex_waitv lands the futex2 multi-wait: up to 128 waiters compared
up front (EAGAIN/EFAULT), WaitOn::FutexV parks the task's array VA,
and futex_wake resolves the hit index under the sleeper's pml4 —
returned in rax like Linux. Absolute timeouts ride MONOTONIC ticks
or wall_now for REALTIME. fchmodat2 gates NOFOLLOW (chmod the link
inode — lstat now reports its stored mode like Linux, born 0777) and
EMPTY_PATH (fchmod-on-dirfd). Audit run-58: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed
in-run; all futex CVE classes are robust/PI/privhash machinery we
don't implement. fxvtest 30 checks green; exit 33, 89 tasks, zero
leak. Ledger at 271 CVEs.
## run-300 — mremap (25) + madvise (28) + mincore (27)

The mm trio lands: mremap does two-pass atomic moves (map+copy
before unmap — shortage rolls back whole), shrink-in-place,
FIXED honoring, and EINVALs a shared-mm move (per-task ledgers).
madvise DONTNEED zeroes present pages (file-private divergence
documented); the rest of the known advice set is accepted-inert.
mincore reports leaf-presence bits with full-byte vec writes.
Findings: the grow pass read src leaves beyond old_end (adjacent
dst aliased fresh pages), mincore OR'd over stale user bytes,
round-up overflow panics on u64::MAX lens, and mincore is NR 27
not 227. Audit run-59: 0 confirmed, 5 fixed in-run. mm3test 30
checks green; exit 33, 90 tasks, zero leak. Ledger at 278 CVEs.

The futex2 op block lands: futex_wait shares FUTEX_WAIT's
compare-then-block with a collapsed bitset mask and an absolute
clockid deadline; futex_wake rides the keyed scan; futex_requeue
does CMP_REQUEUE in futex2 dress — cmpval gate, wake nr_wake, then
rewrites nr_requeue waiters' keys A->B atomically in the syscall
window. membarrier QUERY reports the uniprocessor-safe bitmask;
supported cmds are honest no-ops. The shared futex2_flags_ok gate
also tightened waitv to parse_waitv parity (__reserved==0,
size==U32 exactly). Audit found two gates to add: requeue
src==dst EINVAL (CVE-2014-3153 class) and identical waiter flags
(CVE-2026-31554 class) — both landed. ft2test 28 checks green;
exit 33, 91 tasks, zero leak. Ledger at 281 CVEs.

process_madvise(440) composes with the pidfd family: iovec ranges
validated then per-iov committed-overlap byte counting (holes
advise nothing, like Linux's VMA walk); advice set restricted to
COLD/PAGEOUT/COLLAPSE — all inert here. The mlock family validates
vs the committed ledger and returns — pinning is honest-noop where
nothing evicts; mlock2/mlockall flag-mask EINVALs, and MUNLOCKALL
got its own =>0 arm after the audit caught a garbage-a1 EINVAL on
a no-arg syscall. pmtvtest 27 checks green; exit 33, 92 tasks,
zero leak. Ledger at 285 CVEs.

mseal lands as a real enforcement rung: RNG_SEAL rides the range
ledger's `ofl` through every split (partial ops can't carve
coverage) and all eight layout/content mutator sites gate EPERM —
munmap, mprotect, mremap src+dst, MAP_FIXED-over, madvise
DONTNEED|FREE, brk shrink. cachestat reports VFile residency
(size-clamped page count, everything RAM-resident). Audit caught a
gate-coverage gap — four of eight sites were lost in an aborted
apply; the compiled proof found it. msltest 27 checks green; exit
33, 93 tasks, zero leak. Ledger at 290 CVEs.

run-304 lands the whole classic xattr family in one block: a 48-slot
global XATTRS table keyed by vfile index backs set/get/list/remove
through path (final link followed), l* (link inode kept — no
vfile_real, so lsetxattr EPERMs and the l-reads see the link's own
empty set like Linux), and f* (Ram OFDs resolve through vfile_real
so hard aliases share the inode's attrs). The errno ladder matches
Linux: flags EINVAL, empty/>255 name ERANGE, non-user.* namespace
EOPNOTSUPP-on-set/ENODATA-on-read, size>4096 E2BIG, CREATE EEXIST /
REPLACE ENODATA, links+specials EPERM, short output buffers ERANGE,
table-full ENOSPC. Attrs die with the inode — xattr_purge runs in
vfile_free_if_dead so a reused slot inherits nothing (the C proof
unlinks and recreates to ENODATA). No DAC gate on any of it — the
documented creds collapse (CVE-2010-2071/ksmbd classes out of scope).
xattest 50 checks green; exit 33, 94 tasks, zero leak. Audit run-63:
0 confirmed, 1 hygiene fix (borrow order in fs_setxattr). Ledger at
298 CVEs.

run-305 stacks the xattrat family (463-466) on run-304's table:
four thin wrappers — dfd-relative resolution with the
AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW|AT_EMPTY_PATH mask (everything else EINVAL),
AT_EMPTY_PATH routing a NULL-or-empty path to the dirfd's own
file, and struct xattr_args packed tails parsed with real
copy_struct_from_user discipline (usize<16 EINVAL, >page E2BIG,
nonzero tail E2BIG). NOFOLLOW reuses the l* link-inode semantics;
every tail delegates to run-304's fs_* helpers — no duplicated
logic. memfd_secret (447) rides in as a fourth client of the
extracted memfd_mint_ofd: flags==0 only, born unlinked+sealed
(F_ADD_SEALS refused — secretmem has no sealing machinery);
"secret" is documentary since our RAM fs never aliases file bytes
into kernel maps anyway. Both memfd_secret CVE classes confirmed
absent (direct-map n/a; MAP_SHARED EINVALs so no truncate-alias
panic). xat2test 30 checks green; exit 33, 95 tasks, zero leak.
Audit run-64: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 299 CVEs.
run-306 ships five small classics: select(23), msync(26),
getcpu(309), sched_setattr(314), sched_getattr(315). fd_select
reuses the whole pselect engine — the 16-byte user timeval is
range_ok_w'd once, rewritten in place as a layout-identical
timespec (usec*1000 stays <1e9), and the wait runs through
fd_pselect untouched. The remaining-time writeback is the one
select-specific behaviour Linux owes userspace, and it had to
land at wake time not arm time: a wait_tv/wait_tv_dl ticket arms
before the block and psel_restore — which already restores the
sigmask on EVERY Pselect resume (ready, timeout, signal) —
discharges it, so a timed-out select writes {0,0} and an EINTR
writes real remaining. Sync returns discharge the ticket inline
gated on wait_on!=Pselect so an armed ticket never double-writes.
msync validates like Linux (unknown-flag, SYNC|ASYNC, unaligned
EINVAL; len0->0; ENOMEM on any uncommitted page) then no-ops —
eager private copies never have anything outstanding. getcpu
writes 0s. sched_attr reports the fixed single-CPU normal-class
scheduler: getattr zero-fills then stamps {48,SCHED_OTHER};
setattr enforces the copy_struct_from_user tail (E2BIG),
OTHER|BATCH|IDLE, nice [-20,19], priority+deadline fields 0 —
EPERM/caps absent under the standing creds collapse. fxtest got
an in-run unflake: its phase-2 ctid child EAGAIN'd off phase-1's
stale word=1 and CLEARTID could zero join_word before the SETTID
check read it — word reset so the child blocks, woken after the
check. seltest 36 checks green; exit 33, 96 tasks, zero leak.
Audit run-65: 0 confirmed. Ledger at 300 CVEs.

run-307 lands the SysV SHM API on the SHMSEGS machinery that
MAP_SHARED-anon has used since the shared-memory work: shmget(29),
shmat(30), shmctl(31), shmdt(67). Segments minted through shmget
carry sysv:true and persist at refs==0 until IPC_RMID marks them —
the id dies at mark (resolve EINVALs every verb), the frames die at
the last VMA drop via shm_unref's (!sysv || rmid) gate, Linux's
two-stage removal verbatim. shmids are (gen << 16) | slot with a
per-slot generation bumped at mint, so a freed-then-reused slot
refuses stale ids. shmat resolves the addr rules fully: addr==0
first-fits, SHM_RND rounds down, SHM_REMAP zaps the window like
MAP_FIXED, occupied-without-REMAP EINVALs, RDONLY drops the write
bit and EXEC clears NX. Self-review caught a real UAF: the
partial-map rollback unmapped pages before the VMA registered, so
shm_owns freed segment frames still owned by a live seg — the VMA
now registers first so rollback is shm-aware. shmtest proves real
cross-process sharing (child attach sees parent's marker, parent
sees child's writes), not just errno shapes. MAX_TASKS 96->112 —
shmtest was the refused 97th spawn (pidfdtest hit 88 the same way).
shmtest 30 checks green; exit 33, 97 tasks, zero leak. Audit
run-66: 0 confirmed, 1 fixed in-run. Ledger at 305 CVEs.
run-308 lands a five-classics pack: process_vm_readv/writev
(310/311), copy_file_range(326), clock_settime(227),
fadvise64(221), sched_setaffinity(203). process_vm shares one
engine for both directions — iovec arrays range_ok'd then walked
pairwise through a 4KiB bounce, byte-wise translate_r/translate
on the remote pml4 so unmapped windows EFAULT (only when zero
bytes moved — partial moves return the count like Linux). The
audit self-review caught three real gates: the remote write used
write_task_bytes_force which skips the victim's PTE writable bit
(a peer could scribble another task's read-only pages — now
write_task_bytes, Linux's FOLL_WRITE shape); copy_file_range
validated off_in readable but wrote it back (now range_ok_w);
sched_setaffinity folded a bad mask pointer into EINVAL (now
EFAULT). copy_file_range's self-copy case also got a real fix —
a same-vfile copy's dst growth reallocs the src buffer, so the
Ram src re-resolves per chunk and the copy is memmove.
clock_settime is REALTIME-only writing EPOCH_BASE through a new
rtc::set_wall — wall clock stays monotonic-forward from the
tick. pmvtest proves the real path: a fork'd child arms a pipe,
parent reads its buffer cross-mm, writes through it, child
verifies on exit. pmvtest 28 checks green; exit 33, 98 tasks,
zero leak. Audit run-67: 0 confirmed, 3 fixed in-run. Ledger at
313 CVEs.
run-309 fixes a real numbering bug and lands sigaltstack: the NR
audit found NR_RENAMEAT2=276 — tee's number ran a 5-arg rename
while real renameat2 (316) ENOSYS'd; a full-table sweep found no
other alias, and fs_rename gained RENAME_NOREPLACE (EEXIST on any
live target, EXCHANGE/WHITEOUT get the honest EINVAL). The real
feature is sigaltstack(131) + SA_ONSTACK delivery: three Task
fields hold {ss_sp,ss_size,ss_flags}, sigaltstack validates the
24B stack_t (flags ⊆ DISABLE|AUTODISARM, size>=MINSIGSTKSZ
ENOMEM, ANY modification while on-stack EPERMs), and a shared
sig_frame_sp helper switches the delivery base to ss_sp+ss_size
when the handler is SA_ONSTACK and the interrupted rsp isn't
already inside — AUTODISARM one-shots like Linux. The rung's
test-catch: handler entry rsp was ≡0 mod 16 — a latent ABI break
that #GP'd the first handler using aligned SSE on a local; the
frame base is now ≡8 (x64) / ≡12 (i386). Smaller pieces:
settimeofday(164) shares rtc::set_wall with clock_settime,
syncfs(306) gates fd-kind then inerts, get_robust_list(274)
honestly reports the empty list the kernel keeps. stest proves
real altstack delivery — a handler checks its own frame lands
inside the installed window. stest 24 checks green; exit 33, 99
tasks, zero leak. Audit run-68: 0 confirmed, 3 fixed in-run.
Ledger at 315 CVEs.
run-310 lands the positioned-iovec pair plus four small classics:
fallocate(285) is mode-0-only (FALLOC_FL_* get the honest EINVAL —
no extent machinery) with the same seal gate fd_pwrite uses
(WRITE|FUTURE_WRITE EPERM, GROW EPERM on extension) and ENOSPC at
VFILE_MAX; preadv(295)/pwritev(296) share fd_pxvec — pos<0 EINVAL,
32-iovec cap matching readv/writev, per-segment validation inside
fd_pread/fd_pwrite, checked pos+done. sethostname(170)/
setdomainname(171) write new UTS_NODE/UTS_DOMAIN statics that
sys_uname now reads live (the other four fields stay fixed);
get/setpriority(140/141) replace the pre-existing => 0 stubs with
real gates — which>2 EINVAL, dead-pid who ESRCH, get returns the
ABI's 20+nice=20 since there's no nice field, set clamps and
inerts. fatest proves the lot (grow+zero-fill+ESPIPE/EBADF,
positioned round-trip with untouched file pos, EINVAL/ESRCH
ladders, live utsname readback); lstest floor 72->73, 100 tasks.
The run's interesting bug was in the TEST not the kernel: glibc
syscall() zero-extends int variadic args, so -1 literals arrived
as 4294967295 — correct per the register-level ABI on real Linux
too — fatest passes -1L now. Also fixed a cross-test race
sighted this run: pmvtest/stest both write the shared wall clock
and one's write could land inside the other's assert window; both
windows now cover either epoch. Audit run-69: 0 confirmed.
run-311 lands the pipe-motion trio plus the flagged positioned
pair. sys_splice(275) covers all three directions — file->pipe,
pipe->file, pipe->pipe — with Linux's rules: pipe ends take NULL
offsets only (ESPIPE), file ends use/advance OFD pos or a
validated off_t pointer, one end must be a pipe, same-pipe
EINVALs. The dst-file arm goes through fd_pwrite's seal gate and
vfile_grow; the mid-copy-block divergence is the documented
sendfile shape (short copy or EAGAIN, never sleeps mid-copy).
sys_tee(276) peeks the source ring without consuming — a real
byte-duplicate that proves out against reads on both ends.
sys_vmsplice(278) pushes iovecs into a pipe's write end with
per-segment EFAULT and short-answer semantics. preadv2/pwritev2
(327/328) add the RWF flag gate (SUBSET valid, APPEND maps to
EOF) and pos==-1's use-and-advance-file-pos semantics. sptest
covers all of it; stest's nr-276 probe was updated since tee is
genuinely wired now; lstest floor 73->74, 101 tasks. Two -1
literal test bugs repeated the run-310 lesson (zero-extension
through glibc syscall() is correct ABI — pass -1L). Audit
run-70: 0 confirmed — Dirty Pipe (CVE-2022-0847) is n/a by
construction since rings own private frames and never alias
page-cache memory.
run-312 lands the sched/IO-policy gate pack — fourteen small
syscalls that validate like Linux and return the honest ABI
answer over an inert single-policy core. The sched block:
set/getparam (param pointer gated, nonzero priority EINVALs
since OTHER requires 0), setscheduler (policy must be OTHER —
FIFO/RR EINVAL honestly rather than silently degrade),
getscheduler -> OTHER, priority bounds FIFO/RR->[1,99] else 0,
rr_get_interval writes {0,0} (no quantum exists). ioprio gates
the packed class<<13|data encoding and reports the BE|4
default; readahead is EBADF-or-0 (no cache to warm); tkill
routes to kill(). The NUMA trio validates mode<=4 and the
get_mempolicy flag lattice (ADDR needs NODE, MEMS_ALLOWED
stands alone) before reporting MPOL_DEFAULT + empty nodemask.
schtest exercises every errno ladder; lstest floor 74->75, 102
tasks. Infrastructure: the ESP image went 96->128MiB —
kernel.efi (21MB) + initrd (80MB) silently crossed FAT capacity
this rung (mcopy "Disk full"). Two test-side catches: a vacuous
check (== binds tighter than |) and a flags=3-vs-2 confusion on
the NUMA combo probe — both gcc-visible, both fixed. Audit
run-71: 0 confirmed.
run-313 lands the credentials block plus execveat — seventeen
numbers that make the uid real. Task gains a uid field: spawn
mints 0, the setuid family stores into it (fork/clone/thread
now INHERIT it — the audit caught all three mint sites zeroing
it, which would de-privilege a setuid parent's child), and
getresuid/getresgid write the r/e/s triple with all three
pointers validated up-front. gid/groups stay the root collapse;
setgroups keeps the NGROUPS_MAX+EFAULT gates. capget/capset
speak the versioned header honestly — unknown versions EINVAL
while writing back the preferred 0x20080522, datap fills the
full CAP_LAST_CAP mask for effective/permitted, capset EPERMs
a foreign pid and EFAULTs datap sized to `tocopy`. execveat
factors out of execve: dirfd rides canon_path's Dir-OFD base,
AT_EMPTY_PATH resolves the fd's own /proc link target (the real
path — the earlier synthesized proc/self/fd/N route EACCES'd on
the link's stat until credtest caught it), NOFOLLOW refuses to
