follow. Trap worth recording: NR_GETUID/GETGID/GETEUID/GETEGID
already existed as => 0 stubs — literal arms are silently
shadowed, so impls must land ON the consts. credtest proves the
errno ladders, stored-uid read-back, fork inheritance, and real
rel-path + AT_EMPTY_PATH execs. lstest floor 75->76, 103 tasks.
Audit run-72: 0 confirmed.
run-314 lands SysV message queues — the third real IPC
namespace after pipes and SHM, built on the same skeleton:
(gen<<16)|slot ids, rmid tombstones, WaitOn blocking. The
design rule that makes it safe is Linux's own load_msg
discipline — payloads are staged to kernel memory at msgsnd
entry, so a blocked sender's bytes can't be unmapped or
mutated underneath, and every buffer has exactly one owner
(queue / waker / signal hook). msgrcv implements all four
convert_mode picks including MSG_EXCEPT's EINVAL-on-mtype<=0
and the E2BIG-consumes semantic; blocking senders sleep on a
full qbytes ceiling and blocked receivers wake on send; IPC_
RMID wakes everyone EIDRM and frees queued text. The one
kernel fix the test found: msg_wake_snd was gated on delivery
success, but E2BIG consumes the message — freed room skipped
the wake and stranded the sender. msgtest proves every ladder
plus live blocked-rcv / blocked-snd / RMID-wakes-EIDRM across
real forks. lstest floor 76->77, 104 tasks. The 9-frame leak
in the first run was test-side (queues persist like Linux —
now RMID'd); final-code frames gate deferred on the recurring
wget guest-side stall. Audit run-73: 0 confirmed.
run-315 completes the SysV trio — semaphores land on the same
(gen<<16)|slot skeleton with staged-buffer ownership borrowed
from msgsnd: the whole sembuf array is copied at entry, so a
blocked call's ops can't be unmapped underneath, and every
buffer has exactly one owner (caller / waker / signal-or-tick
hook). sem_try's scratch-val simulation gives Linux's atomic
all-or-nothing landings; the waker re-evaluates each waiter's
staged array on any val change; SEM_UNDO runs a flat
(pid,semid,num)->adj ledger applied at reap with the
[0,SEMVMX] clamp. Two audit catches: stage buffers in slab
classes permanently counted as frame leaks (slab pages never
return — new stage_alloc routes <=4KiB through order-0 frames,
gate now reads zero), and both msgtest/semtest had shared IPC
keys across concurrent suite instances (pid-derived keys now).
CVE-2026-58098 (semop seq-wraparound OOB) is structurally
absent — RMID EIDRMs every waiter before the slot can remint.
lstest floor 77->78, 105 tasks. Audit run-74: 0 confirmed.

run-316 lands POSIX per-process timers — timer_create/settime/
gettime/getoverrun/delete (222-226) on a 32-slot PTIMERS table,
slot-index timerids scoped to the creator's tgid. REALTIME and
MONOTONIC collapse onto the TICKS clock; sigevent is read as
u32 fields with notify, signo and THREAD_ID tid all gated.
ptimer_expire joins the tick service list modeled on
itimer_expire — pend_one delivers SI_TIMER with sigev_value in
si_value, expirations that land while the last notification is
still pending collapse into overrun, and interval timers roll
the deadline past missed ticks. Two audit catches: the
expire-time target lookup checked only pid, so a dead
SIGEV_THREAD_ID target's reused pid could pull a notification
across the process boundary (now requires same tgid), and there
was no exit path for timer slots (ptimer_release_tgid now runs
at exec and when the last tgid member reaps — Linux's
exit_itimers shape). The interval test tolerates Linux's
coalescing: under load a task can sleep through expirations, so
hits+overrun proves rearming. CVE-2017-18344 (unvalidated
sigev_notify -> OOB disclosure) is gated EINVAL; all 20
posix-cpu-timers family CVEs are n/a — no cpu-timer, timerqueue
or sighand machinery exists. lstest floor 78->79, 106 tasks.
Audit run-75: 0 confirmed.

run-317 lands POSIX message queues — mq_open/unlink/timedsend/
timedreceive/notify/getsetattr (240-245) on a 16-slot MQUEUES
table; an mqd is a real OFD so poll/fstat/close all work. Two
ABI traps took real debugging: glibc strips the leading '/'
client-side so the kernel sees the bare name (inner '/' is
EACCES, verified by host strace), and mq_send is NR 242 not
241 — the off-by-one wiring dropped sends onto the receive arm
and produced a phantom EMSGSIZE. Blocking send/receive reuse
the msgsnd block_switch pattern with staged
[prio][len][payload] records (order-0 stage_alloc so frames
stay honest; EINTR and ETIMEDOUT hooks free them). ABSTIME is
CLOCK_REALTIME, converted delta-from-wall_now — feeding raw
epoch seconds to the tick deadline was a never-expires bug.
mq_notify registers once per queue, fires on the
empty->nonempty edge only with nobody blocked, accepts exactly
{0,1,4} like the kernel (THREAD_ID bounded to same-tgid tid),
and clears at reap so a reused pid can't catch a stale
si_value. lstest floor 79->80, 107 tasks, zero leak. Audit
run-76: 0 confirmed; CVE-2017-11176 netlink-UAF class absent
by construction.
run-318 lands waitid(247) — the siginfo-reporting wait API modern
glibc uses through posix_spawn. idtype maps P_ALL/P_PID/P_PGID/
P_PIDFD onto wait4's signed selector (P_PIDFD resolves the fd to a
pid once; P_PID/P_PGID id<=0 EINVAL like kernel_waitid); options
must name an event class. The report is a fully-zeroed 128-byte
siginfo_t via fill_sig_rec — si_signo SIGCHLD, CLD_* code, real
si_uid, si_status. WNOHANG with live children zeroes si_pid so
callers can tell "no change" from a report; WNOWAIT peeks without
consuming (the zombie stays for wait4 to reap). Blocked waits ride
three new WaitOn::WaitId* arms — exit_bookkeeping delivers the
siginfo + rax=0 through the sleeper's pml4 (WNOWAIT leaves the
zombie uncollected), child_change covers stop/continue, and the
setpgid stranded-group rescan handles WaitIdGroup. Two test bugs
surfaced (bad-infop + zero children is ECHILD not EFAULT, and a
WNOHANG-vs-WNOWAIT mask slip in the peek check); the kernel needed
no fixes after the first green run. lstest floor 80->81, 108
tasks, zero leak. Audit run-77: 0 confirmed — the waitid info-leak
family (rusage/uninit-siginfo/io_uring classes) is all n/a.
run-319 lands chroot(161) — the first namespace-flavored rung. FsTab
grows a shared root field beside cwd (CLONE_FS siblings share the
jail, non-CLONE_FS children get a copy). canon takes a floor param:
'..' pops clamp at the root's byte-depth, absolute paths strip '/'
and join the jail as base, and resolve_links re-roots absolute
symlink targets the same way while relative targets floor at the
link's own dir. sys_chroot mirrors sys_chdir (EPERM on uid!=0,
ENOTDIR/ENOENT, cwd NOT moved — Linux keeps the escaped-cwd
semantics). getcwd rewrites jail-relative: cwd==root prints bare
'/', cwd outside prints '(unreachable)/<global-cwd>' — and glibc's
getcwd masks that marker by inode-walking '..' anyway, so the test
uses the raw syscall. Two subtle traps cost real debugging: inside
a jail chdir("/jail/sub") is ENOENT not sub (absolute re-roots), and
the "6-frame leak" turned out to be accounting noise — each
persistent ramfs vfile with data kmalloc(4096)s, which with the
16B header overflows 4096 into KIND_DIRECT order-1 (2 buddy frames
held per file while it lives; freed at unlink). chrtest now cleans
up /jail. lstest floor 81->82. Audit run-78: 0 confirmed — 11-item
escape checklist verified (..' clamp, abs paths, symlink re-root,
proc/self/{cwd,root} resolve inside the jail, sibling-prefix check,
CLONE_FS sharing, escaped-cwd/fd-passing mirrored as Linux does).
run-320 lands a small-classics trio: getdents(78) with the legacy
linux_dirent layout (d_type in the record's last byte, not @18 —
pad-wipe stops one byte short), setrlimit(160) as a prlimit64 alias,
and syslog(103) — a real klogctl reader over the klib::prb ring.
READ blocks on the new WaitOn::Kmsg(seq) until kmsg_expire on the
tick service marshals fresh records through the sleeper's pml4;
READ_ALL/READ_CLEAR/CLEAR and the two size probes round it out, all
content-bearing types gated on uid==0. The blocking proof is a
two-child dance: c1 arms a blocking read, c2 exits, sys_exit's
'pid N exit' printk wakes c1 on the next tick. lstest floor 82->83.
Audit run-79: 0 confirmed — dirent-UAF and rlimit-bypass CVE classes
all n/a by construction.
run-321 lands kcmp(312) — CRIU's kernel-object identity checker.
KCMP_FILE resolves each task's fd through its own fd table into OFD
slot ids (empty fd and idx>=MAX_FDS -> EBADF, matching
get_file_raw); VM/FILES/FS/SIGHAND compare the shared-table
identities pml4/fdi/fsi/sigh; IO always equal (no io_context);
SYSVSEM compares the pid-keyed sem-undo lists. Return contract is
0/1/2 — Linux orders by kernel pointer, we order by table index;
the only guarantee callers use is 0==identical. The access gate
stands in for ptrace_may_access: uid==0 passes, a nonroot caller
needs uid==both targets' uid else EPERM. lstest floor 83->84.
Audit run-80: 0 confirmed — the missing-access-check class is the
only kcmp security class; gate modelled + EPERM-proven by test;
cve-now has no kcmp-class CVEs.
run-322 lands the file-handle pair: name_to_handle_at(303) +
open_by_handle_at(304). f_handle kinds: 1=vfile inode carrying
(slot,gen) — gen minted from a new VGEN sequence on every VFile
create so a dead or reused slot ESTALEs instead of aliasing;
3=root; 4=initrd-archive file by data pointer; 5=dir by canonical
path. name_to_handle_at is ungated like Linux (AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW +
AT_EMPTY_PATH; cap<need -> needed size + EOVERFLOW);
open_by_handle_at enforces uid==0 as the CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH
stand-in, requires an fs-fd mount fd, and decodes strictly — a
handle that survives rename/unlink still opens the inode, while a
mutated or dead one ESTALEs. proc/* and chr/blk/fifo nodes are
EOPNOTSUPP; symlinks take handles on the link inode itself.
lstest floor 84->85. Audit run-81: 0 confirmed — the
mutated-handle class (CVE-2026-99616) is absent by construction,
the access gate is test-proven; no kcmp/handle-ABI kernel CVEs
exist in the catalog. In-run fixes: AT_FDCWD 0xffffff9c form,
proc/self EOPNOTSUPP gap, S_IFLNK node-gate exclusion.

run-323 lands the process-attrs + accounting trio: acct(163),
personality(135), adjtimex(159) — plus clock_adjtime(305), which
is where glibc's adjtimex() actually lands (in-run discovery:
adjtimex() maps to NR 305 with CLOCK_REALTIME, not 159; both arms
share adjtimex_core). BSD accounting: ACCT_VF slot + a fixed
64-byte acct_v3 record appended via vfile_grow inside
exit_bookkeeping for every exiting task (gid collapses to the
unmodelled uid; mem/io/rw/fault comp_t fields zeroed — no
counters exist); acct() itself is uid==0-gated, ENOENT on a
missing file (no O_CREAT), EISDIR dir, EACCES non-regular, NULL
stops. personality stores a per-task persona u32 inherited at
fork — advisory, no syscall-table consumer. adjtimex: modes==0
open query, modes!=0 uid==0 (CAP_SYS_TIME), ADJ_* mask EINVAL on
unknown bits, writable fields stored so set->query round-trips,
TIME_ERROR(5) while STA_UNSYNC / TIME_OK(0) after. clock_adjtime
gates clk==0 — no dynamic-clock path exists. lstest floor 85->86.
Audit run-82: 0 confirmed — both CAP gates EPERM-proven, the
record write is grow-then-fixed-copy, the personality W^X classes
(CVE-2024-47745 / CVE-2016-10044) are absent by construction
(stored-only field, no mmap/AIO consumer, no LSM layer).

run-324 lands unshare(272) plus two honest stubs. unshare
dissociates shared tables exactly like clone's negative flags:
CLONE_FILES/CLONE_FS/CLONE_SIGHAND mint fresh dup'd FdTab/FsTab/
disposition copies then release the old index (dup-then-release —
never a window where the task lacks a table; the sibling keeps
its slot, proven by parent-fd-survives-child-close). VM/THREAD/
every CLONE_NEW* and unknown bits -> EINVAL (no namespaces to
mint); CLONE_SYSVSEM inert (the undo ledger is already
pid-keyed). modify_ldt(154) reads the honest empty default LDT
(funcs 0/2 -> 0, writes EINVAL — the CONFIG_MODIFY_LDT_SYSCALL=n
surface; every LDT CVE class needs an LDT to exist, absent).
pkey trio (329-331) is uniform EINVAL — no PKU. lstest floor
86->87, MAX_TASKS 113->114 (the suite cap caught unstest too —
same class as accttest last rung). Audit run-83: 0 confirmed —
dissociation refcounting is the whole risk surface, and it's
test-proven.

run-325 lands the mount table — the groundwork everything else
(quotactl, statmount, pivot_root) keys off. MOUNTS is 16 slots
seeded rustux-root rw / proc ro+nosuid+nodev+noexec / devtmpfs;
longest-prefix coverage decides who owns a path, mount_ro()
gates every mutation under an MS_RDONLY mount (open-for-write,
mkdir/rmdir, unlink, rename, linkat, truncate+ftruncate, the
chmod/chown/utimens cores, xattr set+remove — fd-path arms
check the inode's name, path cores check the resolved name).
mount() is uid==0, tmpfs-only (ENODEV otherwise), target must
be an existing dir, over-mount EBUSY, MS_REMOUNT re-sets flags;
umount2 is exact-point EINVAL / root EBUSY. /proc/mounts +
/proc/filesystems now generate from the table; statfs/fstatfs
report the covering mount's magic + ST_*; name_to_handle_at's
mount_id is the table's minted id. Divergences: umount doesn't
busy-check open files (removes coverage like lazy umount);
rmdir on a mount point is EROFS not EBUSY; mounts record
ownership/flags only — covered dirs still show ramfs contents
(no shadowing); non-remount mounts don't move the namespace.
lstest floor 87->88, MAX_TASKS 114->115. Audit run-84: 0
confirmed — CAP gate EPERM-proven, EROFS coverage test-proven
on 14 mutation paths, the umount-UAF class is absent (statics,
no superblock lifetimes).

run-326 lands the mount-table consumers: statmount(457) fills
the 512B fixed struct + string tail off MOUNTS (only requested
mask groups get strings, str-relative offsets, EOVERFLOW writes
back the needed size; parent_id is computed on demand as the
mount covering the point's parent dir so an unmounted parent
can't leave a dangling id — root's parent is itself, all
propagation fields 0/private). listmount(458) enumerates
children of a mount with last-id pagination (LSMT_ROOT ->
root's children). quotactl(179) is the honest quota-less
surface: type>2/unknown subop EINVAL, bad special ENOENT,
Q_SYNC no-op, queries ESRCH. Divergences: sb_dev is 0:0 (no
block devices exist); mnt_id==mnt_id_old (no 64->32 aliasing
since ids mint small); parent_id recomputes live so a reparent
shows the current coverer, not the mount-time one.

run-327 lands the detached-mount API: open_tree(428)/fspick(433)
mint mount fds via a new FdKind::Mount carrying the minted mount
id (resolved to a MOUNTS slot at use — a closed+reused slot can
never alias a stale fd). open_tree CLONE mints a private detached
copy; move_mount(429) attaches or moves (F_EMPTY_PATH from an fd
or an exact mount-point path; occupied/root-move EBUSY, under-
self EINVAL, SET_GROUP/BENEATH EINVAL); mount_setattr(442) flips
MS_* flags via the mount_attr struct (set&clr-overlap EINVAL,
single propagation bit, IDMAP EINVAL — no userns, AT_RECURSIVE
walks nested attached mounts). Detached mounts die at last fd
close; fspick is unprivileged like Linux (reference fd only),
the other three are uid==0. Divergences: CLONE copies the whole
covering mount (no subtree slicing — mnt_root stays "/"); moving
a mount doesn't migrate coverage history; propagation stored for
statmount reporting but no group mechanics. mnttest extended in
place (no new task, no MAX_TASKS bump). Audit run-86:
0 confirmed — detached-dissolution/idmap/propagation CVE classes
all absent by construction; stale-fd alias killed by id-not-slot.

run-328 lands pivot_root(155) + the fs-context trio + quotactl_fd:
pivot_root relabels mount records (new mount -> "", old root ->
put_old under it; new must be a non-root mount point, put_old
strictly inside its coverage) — the root-guard everywhere keys on
empty path, not slot 0, since the role travels. fsopen(430) makes
an fs_context fd (tmpfs only, CAP_SYS_ADMIN), fsconfig(431) takes
"source" strings + ro/nosuid/nodev/noexec flag params +
CMD_CREATE (repeat EBUSY, RECONFIGURE EINVAL), fsmount(432) mints
a detached mount + mount fd from a created ctx. quotactl_fd(443)
is the fd-shaped quota-less surface (ESRCH/Q_SYNC/EBADF/EINVAL).
Divergences: pivot is a mount-record relabel — vdirs stay flat-
addressed so covered dirs still show their ramfs contents; cwd/
root strings keep resolving (no dentry pinning); sibling tasks
see the new tree too (their root string covers all); fs_context
has no log output fd side. mnttest extended (same task). Audit
run-87: 0 confirmed — the pivot parent-UAF class needs parent
pointers we don't have; NEWNS class needs namespaces we reject.

run-329 lands the keyctl trio (add_key 248 / request_key 249 /
keyctl 250) — the last self-contained classic subsystem. Real
keys/keyrings: KEYS[32] + KEYRINGS[16] statics, one serial
counter names either; rings hold a u16 bitmask of member key
slots (not a link graph — rings can't contain rings, so no
cycle-detection class exists). add_key mints "user"-type keys
(KEY_SPEC rings lazily resolved: thread=pid process=tgid
session=sid-or-joined user=uid usession=uid group->EINVAL),
dup-desc updates in place, ENOSPC at the cap. request_key walks
the identity ladder narrowed by SET_REQKEY_KEYRING mode and
links hits into dest. keyctl multiplexes 15 ops incl. named
session-ring join (per-sid), describe/read/search/link/unlink/
clear/invalidate/chown/setperm/caps — perm model is uid-match
or the perm word's class bits, foreign keys ENOKEY not EACCES.
Divergences: no instantiation/negation callout (request_key
miss is ENOKEY — no /sbin/request-key), no timeout/gc-delay
schedule (dead keys free eagerly at unlink-to-zero), rings are
permanent once minted (bounded 16, never freed — session_ring
index can't alias), no LSM. keytest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS
115->116. Audit run-88: 0 confirmed — every keyctl CVE class
needs refcounts, ring-in-ring graphs, or unbounded allocation;
all absent by construction.

run-330 lands the system-management tail: sysfs(139) is new —
the vintage fs-registry query — and reboot(169) gains its
missing uid==0 (CAP_SYS_BOOT) gate (a nonroot task could
0xCF9-reset the box; gate sits after magic validation so bad
magic stays EINVAL like Linux). sysinfo(99)/reboot were already
dispatched via NR_ constant arms — the numeric-NR survey scan
missed them. sysfs is the vintage
fs-registry query over a 5-entry table (rustux/tmpfs/proc/
devtmpfs/ext2) — name->index, index->NUL'd name, count.
Divergences: sysinfo loads/shared/buffer/swap/high zeroed (no
load accounting or swap exist); reboot maps every power cmd to
the one chipset reset (no restart-vs-halt distinction in qemu);
sysfs registry is static (ext2 listed since the disk adapter
parses it). systest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS 116->117, lstest
floor 88->90. Audit run-89: 0 confirmed — no Linux-kernel
sysinfo/reboot/sysfs CVE classes exist; the one real risk
(missing CAP_SYS_BOOT) shipped as the fix.

run-331 lands seccomp(317) — a real filter engine. SECFILT[8]
holds immutable installed programs users-counted across fork
(bump at the tasks[] insert, drop in reap); sys_seccomp handles
STRICT (one-way ratchet, nonroot needs PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS)
and FILTER (flag bits EINVAL — no TSYNC/LISTENER infra) plus
GET_ACTION_AVAIL + GET_NOTIF_SIZES. cbpf_validate runs the
classic-verifier checks at install (known opcodes, last insn
RET, jumps in-bounds, scratch <16, ABS loads inside the 64-byte
seccomp_data, W-size only); cbpf_run interprets over a real
{nr,arch,ip,args[6]} record. seccomp_gate sits in both the
64-bit and ia32 dispatchers before the match — strict pends
SIGKILL on anything outside r/w/exit/exit_group/sigreturn,
filter runs the stack most-severe-verdict-wins (KILL_PROCESS
sweeps the tgid, TRAP pends SIGSYS + reports ENOSYS, ERRNO
returns -(data&0xfff), TRACE/NOTIF answer ENOSYS — no ptrace
or listener path exists). Divergences: LOG is a silent allow
(no audit ring), SIGSYS carries no syscall-nr siginfo, notify
unavailable (GET_NOTIF_SIZES still reports real ABI sizes),
512-insn cap vs Linux's 4096. sctest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS
117->118, lstest floor 90->91. Audit run-90: 0 confirmed —
the filter-leak class needs a post-refcount failure path we
don't have; verifier/malformed-prog classes are closed by the
install-time checks + KILL-safe-verdict convention.

run-332 lands Linux AIO (206-210 + 333) — synchronous by
construction. AIOCTXS[8] holds tgid-scoped contexts whose
completion rings live kernel-side (io_getevents is the only
drain — no user-mmap'd ring, which is the documented ABI
divergence; libaio's mmap path would need the ring page).
io_submit runs iocbs inline through the fd layer and posts
io_events; ring-full returns the partial count (EAGAIN when
zero — Linux blocks on its kiocb pool, unresumable past a
block_switch divergence). getevents/pgetevents block on
WaitOn::Aio until min_nr or a relative-timespec deadline;
pgetevents' usig is the {mask,sz} indirection, validated
up-front, installed psel-style so every resume path restores.
RESFD bumps a real eventfd. Divergences: synchronous (no
kiocbs, nothing in-flight — cancel answers EAGAIN for queued
completions, EINVAL for unknown); ring capacity 64; destroy
drops undrained completions; EINTR before min_nr reports -EINTR
(Linux would return a partial count if some events were already
queued). aiotest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS 118->119, lstest floor
91->92. Audit run-91: 0 confirmed — the whole fs/aio.c CVE
cluster needs kiocbs, armed wakes, or a user-visible ring, none
of which exist.

run-333: ioperm(173)/iopl(172) — real I/O privilege. The TSS grew
legs: TssBlock = Tss + 8KB deny bitmap + mandatory 0xFF tail, one
static so iopb's TSS-relative offset stays contiguous; descriptor
limit widened to cover the block (bitmap now live, 0xFF-filled =
deny-all, identical to the old iopb-past-limit default). IOPS[8]
tgid-keyed slots match Linux's io_context shape — CLONE_THREAD
members share the group map, fork copies it (iop_inherit at the
three secfilt_share insert points), last member's reap frees it.
load_iomap runs unconditional at all three switch sites
(launch/switch_to/resume_ctx) — no cache, no staleness window.
sys_ioperm: uid==0 (CAP_SYS_RAWIO), from+num must land in (0,65536]
else EINVAL (Linux's from+num<=from check), bits clear->allow /
set->deny, TSS refreshed in place so the grant applies before the
next switch. sys_iopl: level>3 EINVAL, raising past current needs
uid==0, lowering free; the level rides ctx.rflags IOPL which lands
via sysret's R11 mask (0x3C7FD7 keeps bits 13:12). Audit catch:
rt_sigreturn32 restored frame rflags *including* IOPL — a compat
task could forge IOPL=3 and open every port, a latent escalation
that predates the bitmap; 64-bit sigreturn cleared IOPL outright
(functional regression for legit iopl'd tasks). Both now carry the
task's own IOPL like Linux's FIX_EFLAGS. ioptest.c new task ->
MAX_TASKS 119->120, lstest floor 92->93. Audit run-92: 0 confirmed
beyond the sigreturn fix — no ioperm/iopl CVE classes exist in the
catalog; gate classes reviewed: CAP gate present, bitmap can't
alias (static slots freed only at group death), deny-all default,
exec keeps the tgid map (Linux-true) but drops IOPL via the fresh
ctx — matches rflags semantics.

run-334 — ptrace(101) classic subset (the last big missing subsystem):
tracer/tracee link via Task.tracer + reparenting (ATTACH reparents so
WUNTRACED reports ride ordinary child_change; rppid restores on
DETACH/tracer-death). Signal/fault delivery-stops share one hook in
maybe_deliver: the pending sig dequeues into `inject`, CONT(0)
suppresses, CONT(x) re-pends with a one-time bypass; a traced task's
default-fatal fault stops instead of dying (fault_signal traced
branch). Syscall entry/exit stops via sys_ph + gate pair in both
dispatchers — the entry gate rewinds ctx.rip over the 2-byte syscall
insn so CONT re-executes it under sys_ph, exit gate reports the
result; SIGTRAP|0x80 stop code. SINGLESTEP arms ctx.rflags TF -> #DB
-> delivery-stop. GETREGS/SETREGS/PEEKUSER share a 27-slot
user_regs_struct view (cs/ss kept, rflags IOPL masked — the
reg-forging escalation class is closed by construction). SIGKILL
unsuppressible through ptrace. exec-stop at the new image's entry.
ptrtest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS 120->121, lstest floor 93->94.
In-run fixes: GETSIGINFO needed a wait4-surviving si_signo field;
PTRACE_KILL must work on a running tracee. Audit run-93: 0 confirmed
— 19 ptrace CVEs mapped, all absent-class (no setuid, bounded
translate windows, no utrace/coredump objects, no compat arm).

run-335 io_uring(425/426/427) — synchronous-completion io_uring with
real user-memory rings. FdKind::Uring fd + URINGS[8] table; mmap on the
fd returns zeroed private pages at the real IORING_OFF_* offsets and
records sq/cq/sqes VAs (kernel-side Uring is authoritative for
mask/entries — never the user-writable ring words). io_uring_enter
drains the SQ inline (NOP/READV/WRITEV/FSYNC/POLL_ADD/SENDMSG/RECVMSG/
READ/WRITE, wild array idx skipped, unknown op EINVAL cqe), posts cqes,
bumps a registered eventfd. register: EVENTFD/UNREGISTER/PROBE real,
fixed tables ENXIO. Synchronous model kills the entire
async-request-lifetime CVE cluster by construction. urtest.c new task
-> MAX_TASKS 121->122, lstest floor 94->95. In-run fixes: ring
mask/entries kernel-init at map time (zeroed pages otherwise mean
mask=0 -> avail capped 1); params sq_off is at 40 not 48; probe op
flags are a u16 at +2; masks read from user ring replaced with
kernel-side entries. Audit run-94: 0 confirmed — io_uring class sweep
mapped, all absent-class (synchronous, no fixed tables, bounded idx).

run-336 vhangup(153) — the last unwired classic NR. uid==0 gate
(CAP_SYS_TTY_CONFIG), no-ctty silent 0, real hangup = SIGHUP+SIGCONT
to fg pgrp + session leaders, then CONSOLE_SID drops to unowned. New
piece: /dev/console open by a ctty-less session leader adopts an
unowned console (re-login semantics — TIOCSCTTY stays the console-
session no-op). vhtest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS 122->123, lstest floor
95->96. Suite note: the destructive path is gated to the minimal image
(low pid) — a faithful hangup on the shared console orphans every
sid-1 member's ctty, which broke pgtest's SIGTTIN check when it ran
concurrently. Audit run-95: 0 confirmed — tty-hangup CVEs are all
driver/ldisc races, absent by construction.

run-337 modify_ldt(154) — real per-task LDT, the last stubbed classic
NR. GDT[12..13] holds the 16-byte LDT system descriptor (selector
0x60); load_ldt_of() sets base=&t.ldt/limit=ldt_n*8-1 and lldt's at
all three switch sites (lldt 0 unloads). Task.ldt[16]+ldt_n are raw
descriptor bytes, fork-copied. sys_modify_ldt_real: func0 readback
(ldt_n*8, EFAULT), func2 empty, func1/0x11 writes a user_desc —
entry=-1 first-free alloc with writeback, useable=0 clears, flags
decoded to Linux's type table (contents 0-3 + read_exec_only bit),
DPL3+S1 forced so only CPL3 code/data exists (no call gates -> the
forging class is closed). lldt is re-issued inside the write path —
LDTR caches base+limit so a fresh ldt_n must reload it, not just the
descriptor memory. ldttest.c new task -> MAX_TASKS 123->124, lstest
floor 96->97. Oracles: lsl/verw/mov ds against real LDT selectors
(lsl/verw don't check P on qemu64 — NP needs a real load -> #NP ->
SIGSEGV via in-parent sigsetjmp handler, fork-free); func0 readback
sees the hardware-set A bit (0xf3 vs stored 0xf2). Audit run-96:
0 confirmed — cve-now: CVE-2010-3698 (KVM-LDT, absent), CVE-2015-3290
(espfix, no NMI path). Type-formula fix in-run: 0b1010|x<<1 shared
bit1 so read_exec_only was a no-op -> Linux contents table now.
